1	# 108 — Branch box and predicate routing
2	
3	## Status
4	
5	open
6	
7	## Blockers
8	
9	- 104 (executor must exist; predicate evaluation is added to it here)
10	- 107 (wire UI must exist; branch port wires need visual distinction)
11	
12	## Current behavior
13	
14	No branch box type exists. All routing is linear (one output, one wire).
15	There is no mechanism to conditionally fire one box vs. another based on
16	a value.
17	
18	## Intended behavior
19	
20	A box with "kind": "branch" has named output ports instead of positional
21	outputs. Each port (except "else") carries a predicate. The executor
22	evaluates each predicate against the input value and fires the first port
23	whose predicate is true. If no predicate matches, the "else" port fires.
24	
25	Port predicates:
26	  { "op": "eq"|"lt"|"gt"|"lte"|"gte"|"contains"|"matches", "value": <literal> }
27	
28	"matches" uses lua pattern matching against a string input.
29	"contains" checks whether a string input contains the literal as a substring.
30	
31	Branch box behavior in two scenarios:
32	
33	  Numeric/string predicate branch:
34	    - "else" port must be wired to something, or the runner refuses to start
35	    - If no predicate matches and "else" fires, the program continues from
36	      the "else" port's connection
37	    - The runner logs which port fired for each branch box in last-run.json
38	
39	  LLM output classification:
40	    - "else" port may be left unwired in the box file (omit the connection)
41	    - If "else" fires on an unwired port, the runner re-queues the box
42	      whose output fed this branch box (the immediately upstream box in the
43	      path that led here)
44	    - The upstream box is re-invoked with any fields marked "retry_vary"
45	      in its box file pseudo-randomized (e.g., temperature, top_p)
46	    - Retry limit: configurable per branch box, default 3. Exceeded retry
47	      limit is a hard error, not a silent loop.
48	
49	The "retry_vary" field on a box:
50	  "retry_vary": { "temperature": { "min": 0.5, "max": 1.2 } }
51	  On retry, any field listed here is replaced with a random value in range.
52	
53	## Suggested implementation steps
54	
55	1. Add branch box handling to src/004-executor.lua:
56	   - When a branch box fires, evaluate each port's predicate in order.
57	   - Fire the first matching port's connection.
58	   - If no match, fire "else". If "else" has no connection, trigger retry.
59	2. Add retry state to the executor: a per-box retry counter in run state.
60	   On retry trigger, decrement counter; if zero, halt with error.
61	3. Implement retry_vary: before re-invoking the upstream box, for each
62	   field in its retry_vary table, generate a random value in the given
63	   range and override the corresponding input before invocation.
64	4. Update src/003-loader.lua validation: branch boxes without an "else"
65	   connection are valid only if at least one upstream box has a retry_vary
66	   field. If neither condition is met, the validator rejects the map.
67	5. Update assets/js/006-wires.js: branch box ports each get a distinct
68	   color from a cycling palette. Port labels ("rogue", "wizard", "else")
69	   are drawn alongside the port dot on the canvas.
70	6. Update assets/js/004-inspector.js: branch box inspector shows port list
71	   with add/remove, predicate editor per port (op dropdown + value input),
72	   and retry limit input.
73	
74	## Related documents
75	
76	- docs/001-architecture.md — branch box file format
77	- issues/104 — executor (predicate evaluation added here)
78	- issues/107 — wire UI (port color and label rendering)
79	
80	## Notes
81	
82	Port predicate evaluation order matters: ports are evaluated in the order
83	they appear in the "ports" array. The first matching predicate fires. This
84	is intentional — the user controls priority by ordering ports. Document
85	this in the inspector UI (e.g., "ports are evaluated top to bottom").
86	
87	The retry mechanism is deliberately minimal: it re-runs one upstream box
88	with varied parameters. It is not a general loop construct. If the user
89	needs complex retry logic (backoff, multiple retries with different
90	strategies), they should model it with explicit boxes wired in a cycle
91	through a branch box. The built-in retry is for the common case of
92	"ask the LLM again with a different temperature."
93	
```

## User

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user requested initialization and full implementation of "SoraMech" — a visual FSM/dataflow editor at `/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech`. The system consists of three independent programs sharing a map directory format: (1) a thin HTTP file CRUD server (`soramech-server.lua`), (2) an FSM execution engine (`soramech-runner.lua`), and (3) a vanilla JS infinite-canvas browser editor (`assets/index.html`). The north star use case is LLM orchestration on the SoraMind Alpine cluster. The user explicitly requested: no special cases for any language in the runner, all language drivers use identical shell script interface, server does NOTHING except file CRUD, boxes are each their own JSON file, connections are written to BOTH endpoint files with a 4-field record including `from_box`.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Map directory format**: Each map has `boxes/<id>.json` (one file per box), `data/*.json`, `drivers.json`, `meta.json`, and `tmp/` symlink to `/tmp/<map-name>/`
   - **Connection format**: 4-field record `{from_box, from_output OR from_port, to_box, to_input}` stored identically in BOTH endpoint box files; validator checks exact match
   - **Language driver contract**: `<driver-script> <file-path> <fn-name> <arg-count> [<arg> ...]` → JSON array on stdout; exit non-zero = failure; no special casing in runner
   - **SORAMECH_MAP_DIR global**: Injected by the lua driver shim so box functions can access data files without wired inputs
   - **Branch boxes**: `kind: "branch"`, named output `ports` with predicates (eq/lt/gt/lte/gte/contains/matches), always have an "else" port, use `from_port` in connections instead of `from_output`
   - **retry_vary**: Field on box definition `{"temperature": {"min": 0.5, "max": 1.2}}` — randomized when branch box "else" fires on unwired port
   - **task_fn boundary**: Each box call wrapped in `run_task(box, inputs, drivers, map_dir) -> outputs, err` for future thread pool swap
   - **Thread pool reference**: 3d-rts project issue 127 (frame-ring scheduling) is the target integration; effil-jit available for near-term threading
   - **Atomic writes**: All file writes use write-to-tmp-then-rename pattern
   - **luasocket + dkjson**: Available as symlinks in `libs/`; luasocket serves HTTP; dkjson handles JSON
   - **Vimfolds**: All functions wrapped in `-- {{{ name` / `-- }}}` per CLAUDE.md

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`notes/vision`**: Full project vision document. Three programs sharing map directory. LLM orchestration north star. SoraMind Alpine cluster as target.

   - **`docs/001-architecture.md`**: Updated to include `from_box` in connection format. Map directory layout, box file format (both call and branch), driver interface, execution model, data files format.

   - **`src/001-schema.lua`**: Schema validators for box files, meta.json, drivers.json. Key functions: `M.validate_box(box)`, `M.validate_meta(meta)`, `M.validate_drivers(drivers)`. Updated to accept `retry_limit` on branch boxes. Uses `validate_connection` (requires `from_output`) and `validate_port_connection` (requires `from_port`) — **currently too strict for reciprocal records**.

   - **`src/002-validate-map.lua`**: CLI validator. Key function `validate_map(map_dir)` checks meta.json, drivers.json, all box files, reciprocal connections (4-field exact match, only processes connections where `from_box == id`), branch "else" wiring.

   - **`src/003-loader.lua`**: Graph loader. `M.load_map(map_dir) -> graph, errors` returns `{meta, boxes, entry, drivers, map_dir}`. `M.validate_graph(graph) -> ok, errors` checks reciprocity, driver existence, branch else port, cycle detection (branch boxes are cycle-break nodes).

   - **`src/004-executor.lua`**: Synchronous FSM executor. Key functions:
     - `eval_predicate(pred, value)` — handles eq/lt/gt/lte/gte/contains/matches
     - `run_task(box, inputs, drivers, map_dir) -> outputs, err` — the thread pool swap point; invokes driver via `sh -c cmd 2>tmpfile`
     - `fire_connections(box, outputs, store, queue, boxes, retry_counts)` — branch box predicate evaluation, returns "retry" signal for unwired else
     - `M.execute(graph, map_dir) -> ok, err` — ready-queue based execution; retry_vary now applies randomized values to upstream box inputs before re-queuing; clears branch box inputs from store on retry

   - **`src/005-http-server.lua`**: HTTP/1.0 server module. Dispatch table pattern. All routes: GET/PUT/DELETE boxes, GET/PUT data, GET/PUT drivers, GET/PUT meta, GET /maps. Also serves static assets from `assets/` on GET /. CORS headers on every response. Atomic writes, path traversal guard.

   - **`soramech-runner.lua`**: Entry point. Sets package paths, loads loader+executor, runs load→validate→execute pipeline.

   - **`soramech-server.lua`**: Entry point. Resolves maps_root to absolute path, calls `server_mod.serve(maps_root, port)`.

   - **`drivers/lua.sh`**: Sets `LUA_PATH="${DIR}/libs/?.lua;${LUA_PATH:-;}"` before luajit. Heredoc shim sets `SORAMECH_MAP_DIR` from `script_dir:match("^(.+)/[^/]+$")` (one level up from src/). Calls function, encodes results as JSON array.

   - **`drivers/bash.sh`**: Sources the .sh file, calls named function with args as positional params.

   - **`drivers/c.sh`**: Compiles .c if mtime newer than cached binary in `tmp/cache/<md5-of-path>`. Invokes binary with fn-name as argv[1].

   - **`assets/index.html`**: Single-file app. Dark theme. Canvas (flex: 1) + sidebar (280px). Loads JS in order: 001-canvas, 002-boxes, 003-api, 004-inspector, 006-wires, 005-app.

   - **`assets/js/001-canvas.js`**: Camera state `{x, y, zoom}`. Pan via space+drag or middle-mouse. Zoom via scroll (zooms to cursor). `world_to_screen`, `screen_to_world`. Dirty flag render loop.

   - **`assets/js/002-boxes.js`**: `BOX_W=180`, `BOX_HEADER=36`, `PORT_ROW_H=22`, `PORT_R=6`. `box_height(box)`, `port_positions(box)`, `draw_all(ctx, selected_id)`, `hit_test_port(wx, wy)`, `hit_test_box(wx, wy)`, `get_port_world_pos(box_id, port_name, side)`. Branch ports use palette colors. `Boxes.boxes = {}` is the live store.

   - **`assets/js/003-api.js`**: Async fetch wrapper. `API.init(url, name)`, `list_boxes()`, `get_box(id)`, `put_box(id, data)`, `delete_box(id)`, `get_meta()`, `put_meta(data)`, `get_data(filename)`, `put_data(filename, data)`.

   - **`assets/js/004-inspector.js`**: Autosave on every field change. `Inspector.show(box, on_changed)`, `Inspector.hide()`. Branch box inspector: `mk_branch_port_list` with predicate dropdowns, **now includes retry_limit field and "ports (evaluated top-to-bottom)" section label**.

   - **`assets/js/005-app.js`**: Top-level. localStorage for server URL + map name. `load_map()`, `new_box_at(wx, wy)`, render loop, mouse handlers for box drag, wire drawing, double-click to create box, Delete key for box/wire removal.

   - **`assets/js/006-wires.js`**: Bezier curves with `BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET=80`. `draw_all(ctx)`, `draw_bezier(ctx, x0, y0, x1, y1, color, selected)`. Wire hit testing: 20 sample points, threshold=8px/zoom. `create_connection(from_box_id, from_port, to_box_id, to_input)` uses fetch-before-PUT pattern. `delete_connection(conn)`. Wire selection via click.

   - **`scripts/create-map.sh`**: Creates map directory structure, writes meta.json + drivers.json with built-in driver paths, creates `tmp/` symlink to `/tmp/soramech-<name>/`.

   - **`scripts/test-server.sh`**: 13 smoke tests covering all CRUD operations. All pass.

   - **`maps/hello/`**: Entry box `greet` (no inputs, reads name from data/state.json via SORAMECH_MAP_DIR) → `write-result` (writes greeting back to state.json). Validates and runs successfully.

   - **`maps/driver-test/`**: Three boxes exercising lua (add), bash (stringify), C (uppercase) drivers in sequence.

   - **`maps/branch-test/`**: (IN PROGRESS) `produce-value` (reads n from data/state.json) → `router` (branch box: `low` predicate `lt 50`, `high` predicate `gte 50`, `else` wired to write-low) → `write-low` or `write-high`.

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **dkjson not found in lua.sh shim**: The `require("dkjson")` executed before package.path was set in the heredoc. Fixed by adding `export LUA_PATH="${DIR}/libs/?.lua;${LUA_PATH:-;}"` before the `luajit - ...` invocation.
   
   - **Reciprocal connection validator false positive**: Original `check_connections` flagged connections where `c.from_box ~= id` as an error. But both endpoint files store the SAME record, so the write-result.json legitimately has `from_box: "greet"`. Fixed by skipping connections where `from_box ~= id` with `goto next_conn` — only outgoing connections (from_box == id) are validated by each box.

   - **Leftover dead io.popen call in executor**: `io.popen(cmd .. " 2>&1_STDERR_REDIR_NOT_SUPPORTED")` was printing "Bad fd number" warnings. Removed the dead call; kept only the proper `sh -c cmd 2>tmpfile` approach.

   - **Entry box greet required name input but nothing seeded it**: hello map's entry box had `inputs: [{name: "name"}]`. Nothing wires into it at startup. Fixed by removing the input and having `hello()` read name from `SORAMECH_MAP_DIR + /data/state.json` using the injected global.

   - **Schema validation too strict for branch connection records (CURRENT BUG)**: Running branch-test map fails:
     ```
     maps/branch-test/boxes/router.json: branch connection[1] missing 'from_port'
     maps/branch-test/boxes/write-high.json: connection[1] missing 'from_output'
     maps/branch-test/boxes/write-low.json: connection[1] missing 'from_output'
     ```
     Root cause: branch box validator calls `validate_port_connection` (requires `from_port`) on ALL connections including incoming reciprocal records that use `from_output`. Call box validator calls `validate_connection` (requires `from_output`) on reciprocal copies of branch connections that use `from_port`. Fix needed: distinguish outgoing (from_box == box.id) vs incoming reciprocal connections; validate differently based on source direction.

5. Problem Solving:
   - Resolved how both endpoint files store connections: same 4-field record, validator skips incoming copies (from_box != id)
   - Resolved lua driver's package path bootstrapping via LUA_PATH env var before luajit invocation
   - Resolved SORAMECH_MAP_DIR injection approach: shim derives map root from script_dir (one level up from src/)
   - Currently solving: schema validation incorrectly rejects valid branch box connection records in both branch boxes (incoming regular connections) and call boxes (incoming branch connections with from_port)

162	    local errors = {}
163	    if type(meta) ~= "table" then
164	        return { "meta.json must be a table" }
165	    end
166	    if meta.name == nil or type(meta.name) ~= "string" then
167	        err(errors, "meta.json missing 'name'")
168	    end
169	    if meta.entry_box_id == nil or type(meta.entry_box_id) ~= "string" then
170	        err(errors, "meta.json missing 'entry_box_id'")
171	    end
172	    return errors
173	end
174	-- }}}
175	
176	-- {{{ validate_drivers
177	function M.validate_drivers(drivers)
178	    local errors = {}
179	    if type(drivers) ~= "table" then
180	        return { "drivers.json must be a table" }
181	    end
182	    for ext, path in pairs(drivers) do
183	        if type(ext) ~= "string" or ext:sub(1, 1) ~= "." then
184	            err(errors, "driver key '" .. tostring(ext) .. "' must be a dot-prefixed extension")
185	        end
186	        if type(path) ~= "string" then
187	            err(errors, "driver value for '" .. ext .. "' must be a string path")
188	        end
189	    end
190	    return errors
191	end
192	-- }}}
193	
194	return M
195	
```

1	# 108 — Branch box and predicate routing
2	
3	## Status
4	
5	open
6	
7	## Blockers
8	
9	- 104 (executor must exist; predicate evaluation is added to it here)
10	- 107 (wire UI must exist; branch port wires need visual distinction)
11	
12	## Current behavior
13	
14	No branch box type exists. All routing is linear (one output, one wire).
15	There is no mechanism to conditionally fire one box vs. another based on
16	a value.
17	
18	## Intended behavior
19	
20	A box with "kind": "branch" has named output ports instead of positional
21	outputs. Each port (except "else") carries a predicate. The executor
22	evaluates each predicate against the input value and fires the first port
23	whose predicate is true. If no predicate matches, the "else" port fires.
24	
25	Port predicates:
26	  { "op": "eq"|"lt"|"gt"|"lte"|"gte"|"contains"|"matches", "value": <literal> }
27	
28	"matches" uses lua pattern matching against a string input.
29	"contains" checks whether a string input contains the literal as a substring.
30	
31	Branch box behavior in two scenarios:
32	
33	  Numeric/string predicate branch:
34	    - "else" port must be wired to something, or the runner refuses to start
35	    - If no predicate matches and "else" fires, the program continues from
36	      the "else" port's connection
37	    - The runner logs which port fired for each branch box in last-run.json
38	
39	  LLM output classification:
40	    - "else" port may be left unwired in the box file (omit the connection)
41	    - If "else" fires on an unwired port, the runner re-queues the box
42	      whose output fed this branch box (the immediately upstream box in the
43	      path that led here)
44	    - The upstream box is re-invoked with any fields marked "retry_vary"
45	      in its box file pseudo-randomized (e.g., temperature, top_p)
46	    - Retry limit: configurable per branch box, default 3. Exceeded retry
47	      limit is a hard error, not a silent loop.
48	
49	The "retry_vary" field on a box:
50	  "retry_vary": { "temperature": { "min": 0.5, "max": 1.2 } }
51	  On retry, any field listed here is replaced with a random value in range.
52	
53	## Suggested implementation steps
54	
55	1. Add branch box handling to src/004-executor.lua:
56	   - When a branch box fires, evaluate each port's predicate in order.
57	   - Fire the first matching port's connection.
58	   - If no match, fire "else". If "else" has no connection, trigger retry.
59	2. Add retry state to the executor: a per-box retry counter in run state.
60	   On retry trigger, decrement counter; if zero, halt with error.
61	3. Implement retry_vary: before re-invoking the upstream box, for each
62	   field in its retry_vary table, generate a random value in the given
63	   range and override the corresponding input before invocation.
64	4. Update src/003-loader.lua validation: branch boxes without an "else"
65	   connection are valid only if at least one upstream box has a retry_vary
66	   field. If neither condition is met, the validator rejects the map.
67	5. Update assets/js/006-wires.js: branch box ports each get a distinct
68	   color from a cycling palette. Port labels ("rogue", "wizard", "else")
69	   are drawn alongside the port dot on the canvas.
70	6. Update assets/js/004-inspector.js: branch box inspector shows port list
71	   with add/remove, predicate editor per port (op dropdown + value input),
72	   and retry limit input.
73	
74	## Related documents
75	
76	- docs/001-architecture.md — branch box file format
77	- issues/104 — executor (predicate evaluation added here)
78	- issues/107 — wire UI (port color and label rendering)
79	
80	## Notes
81	
82	Port predicate evaluation order matters: ports are evaluated in the order
83	they appear in the "ports" array. The first matching predicate fires. This
84	is intentional — the user controls priority by ordering ports. Document
85	this in the inspector UI (e.g., "ports are evaluated top to bottom").
86	
87	The retry mechanism is deliberately minimal: it re-runs one upstream box
88	with varied parameters. It is not a general loop construct. If the user
89	needs complex retry logic (backoff, multiple retries with different
90	strategies), they should model it with explicit boxes wired in a cycle
91	through a branch box. The built-in retry is for the common case of
92	"ask the LLM again with a different temperature."
93	
```

### Tool result

```
1	# 109 — Data files: persistent and ephemeral storage
2	
3	## Status
4	
5	open
6	
7	## Blockers
8	
9	- 101 (map directory and tmp/ symlink created there)
10	- 104 (executor must exist; data file access added to driver interface here)
11	
12	## Current behavior
13	
14	No data file access exists. Boxes cannot read or write named data from
15	within their functions. There is no distinction between persistent and
16	ephemeral storage in the map directory.
17	
18	## Intended behavior
19	
20	A map's data/ directory contains JSON files. Each file is a lua table
21	serialized with dkjson. A section-level "constant" flag marks individual
22	fields as read-only. A file-level "constant" flag makes the entire file
23	read-only.
24	
25	Boxes access data files through a lua library (libs/soramech-data.lua)
26	that the driver for lua boxes includes in the lua environment. The library
27	is not built into the runner — it is imported by the lua driver like any
28	other library.
29	
30	  data.get(map_dir, filename, field_path) -> value
31	  data.set(map_dir, filename, field_path, value) -> ok, err
32	  data.load(map_dir, filename) -> table, err
33	
34	data.set refuses writes to constant fields and returns an error string.
35	The caller decides whether to propagate the error or halt.
36	
37	Ephemeral data (state during a run, logs, last-run snapshot) goes to
38	tmp/, which is a symlink to /tmp/<map-name>/. The same library works
39	with tmp/ as a directory argument — no special case. Tmp files are not
40	backed up and are expected to disappear on reboot.
41	
42	## Suggested implementation steps
43	
44	1. Write libs/soramech-data.lua — implements data.get, data.set, data.load.
45	   Uses dkjson for all encode/decode. data.set reads the current file,
46	   modifies the specified field path (dot-separated key), validates the
47	   constant flag, and writes the file back atomically (write to .tmp then
48	   rename).
49	2. Define field_path as a dot-separated key string: "config.model" accesses
50	   table["config"]["model"]. Supports only string keys (no array indexing
51	   in v1).
52	3. Write tests/003-data-test.lua — creates a temp data file, calls
53	   data.set, reads back with data.get, asserts value matches. Tries to
54	   write a constant field, asserts error returned.
55	4. Document in libs/soramech-data.info.md: each public function with
56	   inputs, outputs, and error conditions.
57	5. Update drivers/lua.sh to add libs/ to the luajit package path so
58	   soramech-data.lua is available to any lua box function without
59	   explicit path manipulation.
60	
61	## Related documents
62	
63	- docs/001-architecture.md — data file format and tmp/ symlink
64	- issues/101 — tmp/ symlink creation
65	- issues/102 — lua driver (package path updated in step 5)
66	- issues/104 — executor (data library available to boxes during execution)
67	
68	## Notes
69	
70	Atomic writes (write-to-tmp-then-rename) are important for data files
71	that a running map and the browser editor might both touch. Rename is
72	atomic on POSIX filesystems; the reader always sees either the old or
73	the new file, never a partial write.
74	
75	Constant field validation: when data.set is called on a constant field,
76	return an error string rather than panicking. The driver should propagate
77	this to the runner as a non-zero exit (write the error to stderr). The
78	runner then halts the map with a descriptive message. Prefer hard errors
79	over silent skips here.
80	
81	The library design (separate from the runner, imported by the driver) is
82	intentional. It means the library works identically in a standalone lua
83	script, in a test, and inside a running map. No runner context is needed
84	to use it.
85	
```

dkjson.lua
luasocket
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Lua/LuaJIT language driver for SoraMech.
# Loads a .lua module, calls the named function with JSON-decoded arguments,
# and writes the return value(s) as a JSON array to stdout.
# Contract: <script> <file-path> <fn-name> <arg-count> [<arg> ...]

DIR="/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech"

FILE_PATH="${1}"
FN_NAME="${2}"
ARG_COUNT="${3}"
shift 3

if [ -z "${FILE_PATH}" ] || [ -z "${FN_NAME}" ]; then
    echo "lua.sh: missing file-path or fn-name" >&2
    exit 1
fi

if ! command -v luajit > /dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "lua.sh: luajit not found on PATH" >&2
    exit 1
fi

if [ ! -f "${FILE_PATH}" ]; then
    echo "lua.sh: file not found: ${FILE_PATH}" >&2
    exit 1
fi

# build a JSON array of the raw argument strings for passing into lua
ARGS_JSON="["
SEP=""
for ARG in "$@"; do
    ARGS_JSON="${ARGS_JSON}${SEP}${ARG}"
    SEP=","
done
ARGS_JSON="${ARGS_JSON}]"

# set LUA_PATH so dkjson is findable before the shim runs its first require
export LUA_PATH="${DIR}/libs/?.lua;${LUA_PATH:-;}"

# run luajit with the driver shim inline; the shim loads the module,
# decodes arguments, calls the function, and encodes the result
luajit - "${FILE_PATH}" "${FN_NAME}" "${ARGS_JSON}" << 'LUASHIM'
local json = require("dkjson")

-- read driver arguments
local file_path = arg[1]
local fn_name   = arg[2]
local args_raw  = arg[3]

-- add libs and the module's own directory to the path for any further requires
local script_dir = file_path:match("^(.+)/[^/]+$") or "."
package.path = script_dir .. "/?.lua;" ..
               "/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/libs/?.lua;" ..
               package.path

-- expose map root as a global so box functions can access data files without
-- needing it wired as an explicit input; convention: src/ is one level below root
SORAMECH_MAP_DIR = script_dir:match("^(.+)/[^/]+$") or script_dir

-- decode args array
local args_table, _, jerr = json.decode(args_raw)
if not args_table then
    io.stderr:write("lua.sh: failed to decode args JSON: " .. tostring(jerr) .. "\n")
    os.exit(1)
end

-- load the module
local ok, mod = pcall(dofile, file_path)
if not ok then
    io.stderr:write("lua.sh: error loading " .. file_path .. ": " .. tostring(mod) .. "\n")
    os.exit(1)
end

-- resolve the function
local fn = mod[fn_name]
if type(fn) ~= "function" then
    io.stderr:write("lua.sh: no public function '" .. fn_name .. "' in " .. file_path .. "\n")
    os.exit(1)
end

-- decode each arg from JSON to lua value; strings are passed as-is
local call_args = {}
for i, raw_arg in ipairs(args_table) do
    local val, _, aerr = json.decode(raw_arg)
    if aerr then
        -- not valid JSON — treat as a plain string
        call_args[i] = raw_arg
    else
        call_args[i] = val
    end
end

-- call the function; collect all return values into a table
local results = table.pack(fn(table.unpack(call_args)))

-- encode results as a JSON array (each return value is one element)
local encoded = {}
for i = 1, results.n do
    encoded[i] = json.encode(results[i])
end
print(json.encode(encoded))
LUASHIM
```

2	# Bash language driver for SoraMech.
3	# Sources the target .sh file, calls the named function with args as
4	# positional parameters. The function must echo a JSON value (or array)
5	# to stdout. Arguments are passed as plain strings; JSON decode/encode
6	# is the function's responsibility.
7	# Contract: <script> <file-path> <fn-name> <arg-count> [<arg> ...]

10

12	FN_NAME="${2}"
13	ARG_COUNT="${3}"
14	shift 3
15	
16	if [ -z "${FILE_PATH}" ] || [ -z "${FN_NAME}" ]; then
17	    echo "bash.sh: missing file-path or fn-name" >&2
18	    exit 1
19	fi
20	
21	if [ ! -f "${FILE_PATH}" ]; then
22	    echo "bash.sh: file not found: ${FILE_PATH}" >&2
23	    exit 1
24	fi
25	
26	# source the file to load the function into this shell
27	# shellcheck disable=SC1090
28	source "${FILE_PATH}"
29	
30	if ! declare -f "${FN_NAME}" > /dev/null 2>&1; then
31	    echo "bash.sh: no function '${FN_NAME}' defined in ${FILE_PATH}" >&2
32	    exit 1
33	fi
34	
35	# call the function with remaining args as positional parameters
36	"${FN_NAME}" "$@"
37

1	-- Synchronous FSM executor for the SoraMech runner.
2	-- Walks the graph from the entry box, invokes language drivers for each
3	-- box in dependency order, and threads output values through wires.
4	-- Each box call is isolated in run_task() — the future thread pool swap point.
5	
6	local DIR = "/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech"
7	
8	package.path = DIR .. "/libs/?.lua;" ..
9	               DIR .. "/src/?.lua;" ..
10	               package.path

12	local json = require("dkjson")
13	local M    = {}
14	
15	-- {{{ eval_predicate
16	local function eval_predicate(pred, value)
17	    -- structured predicates only; no arbitrary lua eval
18	    local op  = pred.op
19	    local lit = pred.value
20	
21	    if op == "eq"       then return value == lit
22	    elseif op == "lt"   then return value <  lit
23	    elseif op == "gt"   then return value >  lit
24	    elseif op == "lte"  then return value <= lit
25	    elseif op == "gte"  then return value >= lit
26	    elseif op == "contains" then
27	        return type(value) == "string" and value:find(lit, 1, true) ~= nil
28	    elseif op == "matches" then
29	        return type(value) == "string" and value:match(lit) ~= nil
30	    end
31	    return false
32	end
33	-- }}}
34	
35	-- {{{ shell_quote
36	local function shell_quote(s)
37	    -- wrap a string in single quotes, escaping any embedded single quotes
38	    return "'" .. tostring(s):gsub("'", "'\\''") .. "'"
39	end
40	-- }}}
41	
42	-- {{{ driver_path_for
43	local function driver_path_for(ref, drivers)
44	    -- binary callers (no extension mapping needed — they are executable directly)
45	    -- source callers: look up extension in driver table
46	    local ext = ref:match("%.([^./]+)$")
47	    if not ext then return ref end  -- no extension: treat as binary
48	    local driver = drivers["." .. ext]
49	    return driver
50	end
51	-- }}}
52	
53	-- {{{ run_task
54	-- The task boundary. In v1: synchronous. Future: hand this signature to
55	-- the thread pool. Returns outputs table and ok/err.
56	local function run_task(box, inputs, drivers, map_dir)
57	    local ref = box.ref
58	    local fn  = box.fn
59	
60	    -- locate the driver for this ref
61	    local driver = driver_path_for(ref, drivers)
62	    if not driver then
63	        return nil, "no driver found for ref '" .. ref .. "'"
64	    end
65	
66	    -- resolve ref relative to map_dir
67	    local abs_ref = map_dir .. "/" .. ref
68	
69	    -- build argument list: file fn arg_count [args...]
70	    local args = {}
71	    for _, port in ipairs(box.inputs or {}) do
72	        local val = inputs[port.name]
73	        if val == nil then
74	            -- nil input becomes JSON null
75	            args[#args + 1] = shell_quote(json.encode(nil))
76	        else
77	            args[#args + 1] = shell_quote(json.encode(val))
78	        end
79	    end

141	local function inputs_satisfied(box, store)
142	    -- a box is ready when all its declared input ports have a value in the store
143	    for _, port in ipairs(box.inputs or {}) do
144	        if store[box.id .. "." .. port.name] == nil then
145	            return false
146	        end
147	    end
148	    return true
149	end

153	local function collect_inputs(box, store)
154	    local inputs = {}
155	    for _, port in ipairs(box.inputs or {}) do
156	        inputs[port.name] = store[box.id .. "." .. port.name]
157	    end
158	    return inputs
159	end
160	-- }}}
161	
162	-- {{{ fire_connections
163	local function fire_connections(box, outputs, store, queue, boxes, retry_counts)
164	    -- for branch boxes: evaluate predicates and fire the first matching port
165	    -- for call boxes: push all wired output values directly
166	    if box.kind == "branch" then
167	        local input_val = outputs["_branch_input"]
168	        local fired = false
169	        for _, port in ipairs(box.ports or {}) do
170	            if port.name == "else" then goto next_port end
171	            if port.predicate and eval_predicate(port.predicate, input_val) then
172	                -- find and fire connections from this port
173	                for _, c in ipairs(box.connections or {}) do
174	                    if c.from_box == box.id and c.from_port == port.name then
175	                        store[c.to_box .. "." .. c.to_input] = input_val
176	                        queue[#queue + 1] = c.to_box
177	                    end
178	                end
179	                fired = true
180	                break
181	            end
182	            ::next_port::
183	        end
184	
185	        if not fired then
186	            -- fire "else" port
187	            for _, c in ipairs(box.connections or {}) do
188	                if c.from_box == box.id and c.from_port == "else" then
189	                    store[c.to_box .. "." .. c.to_input] = input_val
190	                    queue[#queue + 1] = c.to_box
191	                    fired = true
192	                end
193	            end
194	
195	            if not fired then
196	                -- unwired else: return signal to re-queue the upstream box
197	                return "retry"
198	            end
199	        end
200	
201	    else
202	        -- call box: push each output value to connected input ports
203	        for _, c in ipairs(box.connections or {}) do
204	            if c.from_box ~= box.id then goto next_conn end
205	            local val = outputs[c.from_output]
206	            store[c.to_box .. "." .. c.to_input] = val
207	            -- enqueue target if all its inputs are now satisfied
208	            local target = boxes[c.to_box]
209	            if target and inputs_satisfied(target, store) then
210	                queue[#queue + 1] = c.to_box
211	            end
212	            ::next_conn::
213	        end
214	    end
215	end
216	-- }}}
217	
218	-- {{{ write_last_run
219	local function write_last_run(map_dir, run_state)
220	    local tmp_dir = map_dir .. "/tmp"
221	    local path    = tmp_dir .. "/last-run.json"
222	    local f, ferr = io.open(path, "w")
223	    if not f then
224	        io.stderr:write("executor: cannot write last-run.json: " .. tostring(ferr) .. "\n")
225	        return
226	    end
227	    f:write(json.encode(run_state, { indent = true }))
228	    f:close()
229	end
230	-- }}}
231	
232	-- {{{ execute
233	function M.execute(graph, map_dir)
234	    local boxes    = graph.boxes
235	    local drivers  = graph.drivers
236	    local entry_id = graph.entry
237	
238	    -- store: keyed by "box_id.port_name" -> value
239	    local store = {}
240	
241	    -- per-box retry counters (for branch box else-retry)
242	    local retry_counts = {}
243	
244	    -- seed entry box: if it has inputs, they must come from data files;
245	    -- for v1, entry boxes with inputs are the user's responsibility to pre-seed
246	    -- via a data box or by starting with a box that has no inputs
247	    local entry_box = boxes[entry_id]
248	    if entry_box and #(entry_box.inputs or {}) == 0 then
249	        -- no inputs needed; ready immediately
250	    end
251	
252	    local queue   = { entry_id }
253	    local visited = {}  -- boxes that have been run (prevent double-execution)
254	    local run_log = {}  -- per-box execution records for last-run.json
255	    local map_ok  = true
256	    local map_err = nil
257	
258	    while #queue > 0 do
259	        -- dequeue
260	        local id = table.remove(queue, 1)
261	
262	        local box = boxes[id]
263	        if not box then
264	            map_ok  = false
265	            map_err = "unknown box in queue: '" .. id .. "'"
266	            break
267	        end
268	
269	        -- skip boxes whose inputs are not yet satisfied (they'll be re-enqueued)
270	        if not inputs_satisfied(box, store) then
271	            goto next_iter
272	        end
273	
274	        if visited[id] and box.kind ~= "branch" then
275	            goto next_iter
276	        end
277	        visited[id] = true
278	
279	        local inputs = collect_inputs(box, store)
280	
281	        -- branch box is handled specially: it doesn't invoke a driver,
282	        -- it routes based on its single input value
283	        local outputs, task_err
284	        if box.kind == "branch" then
285	            -- the branch input is the value wired to its single input port
286	            local branch_val = inputs[(box.inputs or {})[1] and box.inputs[1].name or "value"]
287	            outputs = { _branch_input = branch_val }
288	            task_err = nil
289	        else
290	            outputs, task_err = run_task(box, inputs, drivers, map_dir)
291	        end
292	
293	        run_log[id] = {
294	            inputs  = inputs,
295	            outputs = outputs or {},
296	            status  = task_err and "error" or "ok",
297	            error   = task_err,
298	        }
299	
300	        if task_err then
301	            map_ok  = false
302	            map_err = task_err
303	            io.stderr:write("executor: " .. task_err .. "\n")
304	            break
305	        end
306	
307	        -- fire connections and enqueue newly-ready boxes
308	        local signal = fire_connections(box, outputs, store, queue, boxes, retry_counts)
309	        if signal == "retry" then
310	            local max_retries = box.retry_limit or 3
311	            retry_counts[id] = (retry_counts[id] or 0) + 1
312	            if retry_counts[id] > max_retries then
313	                map_ok  = false
314	                map_err = "branch box '" .. id .. "' exceeded retry limit (" ..
315	                    max_retries .. ")"
316	                break
317	            end
318	            -- find the upstream box(es) that feed this branch box and re-queue them
319	            for src_id, src_box in pairs(boxes) do
320	                for _, c in ipairs(src_box.connections or {}) do
321	                    if c.from_box ~= src_id then goto next_src_conn end
322	                    if c.to_box ~= id then goto next_src_conn end
323	
324	                    visited[src_id] = nil
325	
326	                    -- clear the branch box's input from the store so it
327	                    -- re-fills when the upstream box runs again
328	                    for _, p in ipairs(box.inputs or {}) do
329	                        store[id .. "." .. p.name] = nil
330	                    end
331	
332	                    -- apply retry_vary: randomize specified input fields on the
333	                    -- upstream box before re-running it
334	                    local vary = src_box.retry_vary
335	                    if vary then
336	                        for field_name, range in pairs(vary) do
337	                            local lo = tonumber(range.min) or 0
338	                            local hi = tonumber(range.max) or 1
... [truncated; 200-line cap]
```

/*
 * String operations for driver-test map.
 * Each function is exposed via a dispatch on argv[1] (fn-name).
 * Reads a JSON string from argv[2], writes a JSON string to stdout.
 */
#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <ctype.h>
#include <stdlib.h>

/* {{{ strip_json_string
 * Remove surrounding double-quotes from a JSON string value.
 * Returns a malloc'd buffer the caller must free. */
static char *strip_json_string(const char *s) {
    int len = strlen(s);
    if (len >= 2 && s[0] == '"' && s[len - 1] == '"') {
        char *out = malloc(len - 1);
        memcpy(out, s + 1, len - 2);
        out[len - 2] = '\0';
        return out;
    }
    char *out = malloc(len + 1);
    strcpy(out, s);
    return out;
}
/* }}} */

/* {{{ uppercase */
static void uppercase(const char *json_in) {
    char *s = strip_json_string(json_in);
    int len = strlen(s);
    /* convert in-place */
    for (int i = 0; i < len; i++) {
        s[i] = toupper((unsigned char)s[i]);
    }
    /* output as a JSON string wrapped in an array (driver contract) */
    printf("[\"%s\"]\n", s);
    free(s);
}
/* }}} */

/* {{{ main */
int main(int argc, char **argv) {
    if (argc < 3) {
        fprintf(stderr, "strops: usage: strops <fn-name> <json-arg>\n");
        return 1;
    }
    const char *fn   = argv[1];
    const char *arg1 = argv[2];

    if (strcmp(fn, "uppercase") == 0) {
        uppercase(arg1);
        return 0;
    }

    fprintf(stderr, "strops: unknown function '%s'\n", fn);
    return 1;
}
/* }}} */

## Assistant

Default port is **7700**.

---

**Starting the server, opening the editor, and running maps:**

**1 — Start the HTTP server** (leave this running in a terminal):
```bash
luajit /mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/soramech-server.lua /mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/maps
```
That binds to `http://localhost:7700`. You can pass a different port as the second argument.

**2 — Open the editor in your browser:**
```
file:///mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/assets/index.html
```
Or if your browser blocks `file://` + `localhost` fetch (CORS), serve it through the server itself — the server already serves `assets/` on `GET /`. So just open:
```
http://localhost:7700/
```
In the editor sidebar, set the **Server URL** to `http://localhost:7700` and the **Map name** to whatever map you want to edit (e.g. `classify-demo`). Then click Load.

**3 — Run a map** (separate terminal, any time you like):
```bash
luajit /mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/soramech-runner.lua /mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/maps/classify-demo
```
The runner is completely independent of the server — it just reads the box files, executes the graph, and writes `tmp/last-run.json`. You can run it while the server is up or down.

The phase 1 demo script wraps this for you:
```bash
bash /mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/demo.sh 1
```

## Assistant

Now I have a clear picture. Let me lay out what's broken and what's missing before proposing anything.

**Broken today (explains why boxes don't show):**

`005-app.js` does `Boxes.boxes = {}` on load — this *replaces* the module property with a new object, but `draw_all` inside the closure still reads from the *original* empty object. The boxes load from the server and go into the new object; the renderer never sees them. The fix is to mutate the existing object (clear then repopulate) instead of replacing it.

**What exists but isn't obvious:**
- Double-click empty canvas → create box
- Drag an output port dot → draw wire → release on input port
- Click wire → select it → Delete key removes it
- Inspector autosaves on every keystroke

The user can't discover any of this because there are no visible boxes to interact with.

---

**Proposed Phase 2 — "Editor you can actually use"**

Six issues in priority order:

| ID | Title | Why |
|----|-------|-----|
| 201 | Fix box rendering closure bug | Boxes are loaded but invisible — nothing else works until this is fixed |
| 202 | Fit-to-view on map load | Once boxes render, they may be off-screen at origin; fit-to-view centers them |
| 203 | Map picker panel | Typing map names into a browser prompt is painful; replace with a list from the server |
| 204 | Visible interaction hints | User has no way to know double-click creates boxes or how wiring works |
| 205 | Run button + last-run status | Show the runner output in the editor so you don't need a separate terminal |
| 206 | Entry box designation | Editor should show which box is the entry point and let you change it |

I'd skip data file editing for Phase 2 — it's useful but the six above are what makes the tool feel operational vs. broken.

Does this match what you're thinking, or would you reorder / cut anything?

## Assistant

**What ref and fn do:**

- **ref** — the path to the source file, relative to the map root. E.g. `src/greet.lua`, `src/stamp.sh`. This tells the runner which file to pass to the language driver.
- **fn** — the function name within that file to call. E.g. `hello`, `write_result`. The runner passes this as the second argument to the driver.

Together they fully specify one invocation: *"run the function `hello` from `src/greet.lua` using whatever driver handles `.lua` files."*

---

**On inputs/outputs:**

You're right, and this points to a real design gap. Right now the inspector lets you type port names and types by hand — but the function already *has* a signature. The box is just a wiring point for that function. Letting the user invent port names that don't match the function is a source of errors with no feedback.

The project convention (from CLAUDE.md) already calls for a `.info.md` sidecar next to each source file listing its public functions and their inputs/outputs. `soramech-data.info.md` is an example we wrote. That file is the natural source of truth for the editor.

The revised Phase 2 plan, with 205 dropped and this folded in:

| ID | Title |
|----|-------|
| 201 | Fix box rendering closure bug |
| 202 | Fit-to-view on map load |
| 203 | Map picker panel |
| 204 | Visible interaction hints |
| 205 | ~~Run button~~ — dropped |
| 206 | Entry box designation |
| 207 | Auto-derive ports from source file manifest (ref+fn → read info.md → populate ports read-only) |

207 needs a server endpoint to read `src/*.info.md` files, and a convention for the info.md format that the editor can parse. How are you thinking about that? Should the info.md files be the mechanism, or would you prefer something else — a doc comment in the source file, a JSON sidecar, a separate schema file?

36	    f:write(data)
37	    f:close()
38	    local ok, rerr = os.rename(tmp, path)
39	    if not ok then return false, rerr end
40	    return true
41	end
42	-- }}}
43	
44	-- {{{ parse_json_file
45	local function parse_json_file(path)
46	    local raw, err = read_file(path)
47	    if not raw then return nil, err end
48	    local obj, _, jerr = json.decode(raw)
49	    if not obj then return nil, "JSON error: " .. tostring(jerr) end
50	    return obj
51	end
52	-- }}}
53	
54	-- {{{ list_dir_json
55	local function list_dir_json(dir)
56	    local handle = io.popen("ls " .. dir .. "/*.json 2>/dev/null")
57	    if not handle then return {} end
58	    local listing = handle:read("*a")
59	    handle:close()
60	    local files = {}
61	    for path in listing:gmatch("[^\n]+") do
62	        files[#files + 1] = path
63	    end
64	    return files
65	end
66	-- }}}
67	
68	-- {{{ list_subdirs
69	local function list_subdirs(dir)
70	    local handle = io.popen("ls -d " .. dir .. "/*/ 2>/dev/null")
71	    if not handle then return {} end
72	    local listing = handle:read("*a")
73	    handle:close()
74	    local names = {}
75	    for path in listing:gmatch("[^\n]+") do
76	        local name = path:match("/([^/]+)/$")
77	        if name then names[#names + 1] = name end
78	    end
79	    return names
80	end
81	-- }}}
82	
83	-- {{{ safe_path
84	-- Returns the resolved path if it is within root, nil otherwise.
85	-- Prevents path traversal outside the maps root.
86	local function safe_path(root, ...)
87	    local parts = { root }
88	    for _, p in ipairs({...}) do
89	        -- reject any component containing ".."
90	        if tostring(p):find("%.%.", 1, true) then return nil end
91	        parts[#parts + 1] = p
92	    end
93	    return table.concat(parts, "/")
94	end
95	-- }}}
96	
97	-- {{{ find_references
98	-- Scan all box files in a map for any connection referencing the given box id.
99	local function find_references(map_dir, box_id)
100	    local refs = {}
101	    local files = list_dir_json(map_dir .. "/boxes")
102	    for _, path in ipairs(files) do
103	        local box, _ = parse_json_file(path)
104	        if box and box.id ~= box_id then
105	            for _, c in ipairs(box.connections or {}) do
106	                if c.to_box == box_id or c.from_box == box_id then
107	                    refs[#refs + 1] = box.id
108	                    break
109	                end
110	            end
111	        end
112	    end
113	    return refs
114	end
115	-- }}}
116	
117	-- {{{ respond
118	local function respond(client, status, body, content_type)
119	    content_type = content_type or "application/json"
120	    local response = table.concat({
121	        "HTTP/1.0 " .. status,
122	        "Content-Type: " .. content_type,
123	        "Content-Length: " .. #body,
124	        "Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *",
125	        "Access-Control-Allow-Methods: GET, PUT, DELETE, OPTIONS",
126	        "Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Content-Type",
127	        "Connection: close",
128	        "",
129	        body,
130	    }, "\r\n")
131	    client:send(response)
132	end
133	-- }}}
134	
135	-- {{{ json_ok
136	local function json_ok(client, data)
137	    respond(client, "200 OK", json.encode(data))
138	end
139	-- }}}
140	
141	-- {{{ json_err
142	local function json_err(client, status, msg)
143	    respond(client, status, json.encode({ error = msg }))
144	end
145	-- }}}
146	
147	-- {{{ parse_request
148	local function parse_request(client)
149	    local line, err = client:receive("*l")
150	    if not line then return nil, err end
151	
152	    local method, path, _ = line:match("^(%u+) ([^ ]+) HTTP")
153	    if not method then return nil, "bad request line: " .. line end
154	
155	    -- read headers
156	    local headers = {}
157	    local content_length = 0
158	    while true do
159	        local hline = client:receive("*l")
160	        if not hline or hline == "" then break end
161	        local k, v = hline:match("^([^:]+):%s*(.+)")
162	        if k then
163	            headers[k:lower()] = v
164	            if k:lower() == "content-length" then
165	                content_length = tonumber(v) or 0
166	            end
167	        end
168	    end
169	
170	    -- read body if present
171	    local body = ""
172	    if content_length > 0 then
173	        body = client:receive(content_length) or ""
174	    end
175	
176	    -- split path into segments
177	    local parts = {}
178	    for seg in path:gmatch("[^/]+") do parts[#parts + 1] = seg end
179	
180	    return { method = method, path = path, parts = parts, body = body }
181	end
182	-- }}}
183	
184	-- {{{ handle_options
185	local function handle_options(client)
186	    respond(client, "200 OK", "", "text/plain")
187	end
188	-- }}}
189	
190	-- ============================================================
191	-- Route handlers
192	-- ============================================================
193	
194	-- {{{ handle_list_maps
195	local function handle_list_maps(client, req, maps_root)
196	    local names = list_subdirs(maps_root)
197	    json_ok(client, names)
198	end
199	-- }}}
200

**Boxes rendering**: fixed — the boxes from `hello` map will now appear when you load.

  hello()
    in:  (none)
    out: result

── src/result-writer.lua
  write_result(text)
    in:  text
    out: (none)
```

Click a function and it sets ref, fn, inputs, and outputs on the box automatically. No manual port typing needed.

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The session continued from a prior context. The project is SoraMech — a visual FSM/dataflow editor with three independent programs: an HTTP file CRUD server (`soramech-server.lua`), an FSM execution engine (`soramech-runner.lua`), and a vanilla JS browser editor (`assets/index.html`). The north star use case is LLM orchestration.

   Across this session, the user requested:
   - Fix issue 108 (branch box schema validation rejecting valid connection records)
   - Fix issue 109 (implement `libs/soramech-data.lua` — data.get, data.set, data.load)
   - Fix issue 110 (phase 1 demo — classify-demo map)
   - Fix issue 111 (bash driver output format; driver-test silent skip)
   - Scripts to start the server and open Firefox
   - Plan Phase 2 of the editor
   - Explicitly DROP issue 205 (run button): "the editor should have no concept of the map files running. It's simply an editor that creates programs. Less of an IDE, and more of a text editor."
   - Source file browser in the inspector: "Can we create a simple file browser that lets a user pick a specific file, and upon picking it can we show a list in the panel on the right of all the functions in the file, with an indented newline for each of the inputs and output values?"
   - Inputs/outputs derived from source files NOT manually editable: "just, look through the file for all return statements. Look at the function signature for inputs."
   - Info.md NOT required: "the user is expected to write their own src files, so there's no guarantee that an info.md file will exist"
   - Write all remaining phase 2 issue files (202, 203, 204, 206)
   - Three fixes currently in progress: (1) delete box button, (2) browse src/ failing, (3) ref field should be editable not read-only

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Map directory format: `boxes/<id>.json`, `data/*.json`, `drivers.json`, `meta.json`, `tmp/` symlink
   - Connection format: 4-field record `{from_box, from_output OR from_port, to_box, to_input}` stored identically in BOTH endpoint box files
   - Language driver contract: `<driver> <file> <fn> <arg_count> [args...]` → JSON array on stdout where each element is itself a JSON-encoded value
   - SORAMECH_MAP_DIR global injected by lua driver shim so box functions can locate data files
   - Branch boxes: `kind: "branch"`, named output `ports` with predicates (eq/lt/gt/lte/gte/contains/matches), always have "else" port
   - Data file format: `{constant: bool, fields: {key: {constant: bool, value: any}}}`
   - Atomic writes: write to `.tmp` then `os.rename`
   - JS module pattern (IIFE): all editor modules use `const Mod = (() => { ... return {...}; })();`
   - Closure reference bug: reassigning `Boxes.boxes = {}` breaks the closure; must mutate the existing object instead
   - Server routes use a dispatch table pattern matching method + path parts array
   - File browser parses Lua (`function M.name(args)` + return statement scanning) and Bash (`name() {` + `local var="${N}"` scanning)

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`src/001-schema.lua`**
     - Added `validate_incoming_connection` function accepting either `from_output` or `from_port`
     - Changed `validate_box` to route by direction: `if c.from_box == box.id` → kind-specific validator, else → incoming validator
     ```lua
     local function validate_incoming_connection(c, i, errors)
         if c.from_box == nil then err(errors, "connection[" .. i .. "] missing 'from_box'") end
         if c.from_output == nil and c.from_port == nil then
             err(errors, "connection[" .. i .. "] missing both 'from_output' and 'from_port'")
         end
         if c.to_box == nil then err(errors, "connection[" .. i .. "] missing 'to_box'") end
         if c.to_input == nil then err(errors, "connection[" .. i .. "] missing 'to_input'") end
     end
     ```

   - **`libs/soramech-data.lua`**
     - New file implementing M.load, M.get, M.set
     - Field path: dot-separated string navigating `{constant, value}` wrapper structure
     - Constant enforcement at file-level and field-level
     - Atomic writes via write-to-.tmp-then-os.rename

   - **`tests/003-data-test.lua`**
     - 9 unit tests: load table, get numeric, get string, set round-trip, constant field rejection, file-level constant rejection, missing field error, missing file error. All pass.

   - **`maps/classify-demo/`** — Phase 1 demo map
     - 5-box pipeline: `read-input` (lua) → `stamp` (bash) → `router` (branch, `contains "SoraMech"`) → `write-result` or `write-error` (both lua)
     - Bash `stamp` function uses `printf '["%s"]\n'` format (correct JSON array output)

   - **`maps/driver-test/`** — Fixed
     - lua-box.json: removed wired inputs a,b
     - `src/math-funcs.lua`: `add()` now reads from `data/state.json` via `SORAMECH_MAP_DIR`
     - `data/state.json`: created with a=3, b=5
     - `src/utils.sh` stringify: changed from `echo "\"${value}\""` to `printf '["%s"]\n' "stringified: ${value}"`

   - **`scripts/start-server.sh`** — starts server at DIR/maps on port 7700 (configurable)

   - **`scripts/open-editor.sh`** — opens Firefox at server URL, checks for firefox on PATH

   - **`src/005-http-server.lua`**
     - Added `handle_list_src`: GET /maps/:name/src → lists files in src/ directory
     - Added `handle_get_src`: GET /maps/:name/src/:file → returns file as text/plain
     - Added both routes to dispatch at lines 400 and 405

   - **`assets/js/003-api.js`**
     - Added `list_src_files()` → GET /maps/:name/src
     - Added `get_src_file(filename)` → GET /maps/:name/src/:filename

   - **`assets/js/007-filebrowser.js`** — new file
     - `parse_lua_returns(body)`: scans for `return` statements, uses bare identifier names as output port names, falls back to "result" for single complex expressions
     - `parse_lua(content)`: matches `function M.name(args)` pattern, extracts function bodies by position, calls parse_lua_returns
     - `parse_bash_inputs(body)`: looks for `local var="${N}"` patterns; falls back to counting `$N` references
     - `parse_bash(content)`: matches `name() {` pattern
     - `parse_functions(content, filename)`: dispatches by file extension (.lua, .sh, null for others)
     - `render(container, on_select)`: async; fetches file list → show files → on click fetch content, parse, show functions; on function click calls on_select(filename, fn)

   - **`assets/js/004-inspector.js`** — significantly rewritten
     - Added `on_delete_cb` variable
     - `show(box, on_changed, on_delete)` — new third parameter
     - Delete button added at top of inspector when on_delete_cb is provided:
       ```javascript
       const del_btn = document.createElement('button');
       del_btn.textContent = 'delete box';
       del_btn.className   = 'delete-box-btn';
       del_btn.onclick     = () => on_delete_cb();
       fields.appendChild(del_btn);
       ```
     - ref field changed to read-only display + Browse button (browse opens FileBrowser.render)
     - fn field: read-only display (`mk_readonly`)
     - inputs/outputs: read-only `port-display` divs (not editable)
     - Branch boxes: manual inputs still editable; ports still editable
     - `open_browser()`: replaces inspector content with file browser; on function select sets ref/fn/inputs/outputs and saves

   - **`assets/js/005-app.js`**
     - Fixed closure bug: `Object.keys(Boxes.boxes).forEach(k => delete Boxes.boxes[k])` instead of `Boxes.boxes = {}`
     - Extracted `delete_box(id)` function (shared by Delete key and inspector button)
     - Exposed as `window.App_delete_box`
     - Updated `new_box_at` and box-click handler to pass `() => delete_box(id)` as third arg to `Inspector.show()`

   - **`assets/index.html`**
     - Added CSS for `.field-readonly`, `.port-display`, `.port-display-row`, `.port-display-empty`, `.fb-header`, `.fb-back`, `.fb-file-row`, `.fb-fn-row`, `.fb-fn-name`, `.fb-fn-ports`, `.fb-note`
     - Added `<script src="js/007-filebrowser.js"></script>` (before 004-inspector.js)
     - Still needs: `.delete-box-btn` CSS styling

   - **`issues/202-fit-to-view-on-map-load.md`** — written, open
   - **`issues/203-map-picker-panel.md`** — written, open
   - **`issues/204-visible-interaction-hints.md`** — written, open
   - **`issues/206-entry-box-designation.md`** — written, open
   - **`issues/phase-2-progress.md`** — created tracking all 6 phase 2 issues

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **Issue 108 schema validation**: Both branch and call box files contain reciprocal connection records using the other kind's field (`from_port` vs `from_output`). Fixed by routing validation on direction (`c.from_box == box.id`) rather than box kind.
   - **driver-test silent skip**: Entry box had wired inputs that were never seeded, so inputs_satisfied always returned false and nothing ran. Map reported "OK" with empty boxes log. Fixed by making add() read from data file with no wired inputs.
   - **bash stringify wrong format**: Output `"42"` (bare JSON string), executor expected JSON array `[...]`. Fixed to `printf '["%s"]\n' "stringified: ${value}"`. The C box already used the correct format.
   - **Box closure reference bug**: `Boxes.boxes = {}` replaced the module property but draw_all closure read the original empty variable. Fixed by mutating: `Object.keys(Boxes.boxes).forEach(k => delete Boxes.boxes[k])`.
   - **Browse src/ returns "no route"**: Server was started before the new routes were added. Server must be restarted. The route code is correct (lines 400-405 in dispatch table).
   - **User feedback on issue 205**: User said to drop it — editor should not know about running maps.
   - **User feedback on inputs/outputs**: Should not be manually editable — derived from source file. Info.md not reliable since users write their own files; instead parse return statements and function signature directly.
   - **User feedback on ref field**: Should be editable text input, not just a browse button. "I should be able to type into the ref box or click the browse button. Just in-case I know the file path by heart."

1	// Source file browser and function signature parser.

4	const FileBrowser = (() => {
5	
6	  // {{{ parse_lua_returns
7	  // Scans a function body for return statements; returns an array of output names.
8	  function parse_lua_returns(body) {
9	    const re    = /\breturn\b\s*([^\n]+)/g;
10	    const names = new Set();
11	    let m;
12	    while ((m = re.exec(body)) !== null) {
13	      const expr = m[1].replace(/--.*$/, '').trim();
14	      if (!expr) continue;
15	      expr.split(',').forEach((part, i) => {
16	        part = part.trim();
17	        // bare identifier → use as port name
18	        if (/^[a-zA-Z_]\w*$/.test(part) &&
19	            !['nil','true','false','not','and','or','end'].includes(part)) {
20	          names.add(part);
21	        } else if (expr.split(',').length === 1 && part) {
22	          // single complex expression → call it "result"
23	          names.add('result');
24	        }
25	        // multi-value complex expression: skip unnamed parts
26	      });
27	    }
28	    return [...names];
29	  }
30	  // }}}
31	
32	  // {{{ parse_lua
33	  function parse_lua(content) {
34	    const fns    = [];
35	    const fn_re  = /^function\s+M\.(\w+)\s*\(([^)]*)\)/mg;
36	    const starts = [];
37	    let m;
38	    while ((m = fn_re.exec(content)) !== null) {
39	      starts.push({ name: m[1], args: m[2], body_start: m.index + m[0].length });
40	    }
41	    starts.forEach((fn, i) => {
42	      const body_end = i + 1 < starts.length ? starts[i + 1].body_start : content.length;
43	      const body     = content.slice(fn.body_start, body_end);
44	      const inputs   = fn.args.trim()
45	        ? fn.args.split(',').map(s => s.trim()).filter(Boolean)
46	        : [];
47	      const outputs  = parse_lua_returns(body);
48	      fns.push({ name: fn.name, inputs, outputs });
49	    });
50	    return fns;
51	  }
52	  // }}}
53	
54	  // {{{ parse_bash_inputs
55	  function parse_bash_inputs(body) {
56	    const params = {};
57	    // local var="${N}" or local var="$N"
58	    const local_re = /local\s+(\w+)\s*=\s*["']?\$\{?(\d+)\}?["']?/g;
59	    let m;
60	    while ((m = local_re.exec(body)) !== null) {
61	      params[parseInt(m[2])] = m[1];
62	    }
63	    if (Object.keys(params).length > 0) {
64	      const max = Math.max(...Object.keys(params).map(Number));
65	      return Array.from({ length: max }, (_, i) => params[i + 1] || `arg${i + 1}`);
66	    }
67	    // fall back: scan for raw $N references
68	    const seen = new Set();
69	    const pos_re = /\$\{?(\d+)\}?/g;
70	    while ((m = pos_re.exec(body)) !== null) {
71	      const n = parseInt(m[1]);
72	      if (n > 0) seen.add(n);
73	    }
74	    if (seen.size > 0) {
75	      const max = Math.max(...seen);
76	      return Array.from({ length: max }, (_, i) => `arg${i + 1}`);
77	    }
78	    return [];
79	  }
80	  // }}}
81	
82	  // {{{ parse_bash
83	  function parse_bash(content) {
84	    const fns   = [];
85	    const re    = /^(\w+)\s*\(\s*\)\s*\{/mg;
86	    const skip  = new Set(['if','while','for','case','do','done','fi','then','else']);
87	    let m;
88	    while ((m = re.exec(content)) !== null) {
89	      const name = m[1];
90	      if (skip.has(name)) continue;
91	      const body_start = m.index + m[0].length;
92	      const close      = content.indexOf('\n}', body_start);
93	      const body       = content.slice(body_start, close > -1 ? close : body_start + 800);
94	      const inputs     = parse_bash_inputs(body);
95	      fns.push({ name, inputs, outputs: ['output'] });
96	    }
97	    return fns;
98	  }
99	  // }}}
100	
101	  // {{{ parse_functions
102	  // Returns [{name, inputs:[string], outputs:[string]}] for a given file.
103	  function parse_functions(content, filename) {
104	    const ext = filename.split('.').pop();
105	    if (ext === 'lua') return parse_lua(content);
106	    if (ext === 'sh')  return parse_bash(content);
107	    return null;  // null = no parser for this extension
108	  }
109	  // }}}
110

-- Hello function for the hello example map.
-- Reads the name from data/state.json and returns a greeting string.
-- No inputs: name comes from the map's data file at runtime.

local json = require("dkjson")
local M    = {}

-- {{{ read_name
local function read_name(map_dir)
    -- walk up from this file's location to find the map root's data/state.json
    local state_path = map_dir .. "/data/state.json"
    local f = io.open(state_path, "r")
    if not f then return "world" end
    local raw  = f:read("*a")
    f:close()
    local obj, _, _ = json.decode(raw)
    if not obj then return "world" end
    local name_field = obj.fields and obj.fields.name
    if not name_field then return "world" end
    return name_field.value or "world"
```

## Assistant

Good. No server restart needed — reload the page.

---

**On per-box values:** you're right that parsing variable definitions from arbitrary downloaded libraries is a non-starter. Two approaches worth considering:

**Option A — Manual "config" table on the box.** Add a `box.config` field (key-value pairs, editable in the inspector). At runtime the runner injects them as a Lua global table before calling the function, or passes them as an extra argument. Works with any library, zero source parsing needed. The user decides which knobs exist.

**Option B — Use the existing data/ system.** Each box can already read from `data/*.json` files at runtime via `SORAMECH_MAP_DIR`. The inspector could just show a "linked data file" picker. Less editor overhead, already implemented.

My recommendation is **Option A** — a lightweight `box.config` section in the inspector (a simple add/remove key-value list, same pattern as the branch ports UI). It's self-contained per box, visible at a glance, and doesn't require the user to know the data/ directory layout. The runner side just needs one extra injected global before the function call.

Want me to sketch the runner injection side so you can decide if the contract makes sense before building the UI?

## Assistant

Good call — narrowing scope to your actual use case is the right move.

Here's the convention I'd propose for Lua:

```lua
function M.classify(text)
  local values = {
    keyword        = "SoraMech",
    case_sensitive = false,
    max_retries    = 3,
  }
  -- rest of function uses values.keyword, etc.
  return result
end
```

The parser already scans the function body — it just needs to also look for `local values = {` and extract the key/default-value pairs. The inspector shows them as editable fields. The box stores overrides in `box.values`. At runtime, the Lua driver sets a `SORAMECH_VALUES` global with the box's overrides before calling the function, and you write:

```lua
local values = SORAMECH_VALUES or {
  keyword = "SoraMech",
  ...
}
```

Or we add a one-liner helper to `libs/soramech-data.lua` so you don't write that every time:

```lua
local values = sora.values({ keyword = "SoraMech", ... })
-- (merges SORAMECH_VALUES overrides on top of your defaults)
```

For **C** it's harder since it's compiled. The natural approach there is: the C function reads a config file from `SORAMECH_MAP_DIR/tmp/<box_id>_values.json` that the runner writes before invoking it. Slightly more boilerplate, but the pattern is the same.

My honest take: this is a solid convention. It's readable, self-documenting, and the defaults live right next to the code that uses them. The one thing to decide before building it is the variable name — `values` vs `config` vs `params`. I'd lean toward `config` since `values` is a common local variable name that might collide accidentally.

Should I start with the parser side (extract `local config = {` blocks in the file browser) or the runner injection side first?

16	local function eval_predicate(pred, value)
17	    -- structured predicates only; no arbitrary lua eval
18	    local op  = pred.op
19	    local lit = pred.value
20	
21	    if op == "eq"       then return value == lit
22	    elseif op == "lt"   then return value <  lit
23	    elseif op == "gt"   then return value >  lit
24	    elseif op == "lte"  then return value <= lit
25	    elseif op == "gte"  then return value >= lit
26	    elseif op == "contains" then
27	        return type(value) == "string" and value:find(lit, 1, true) ~= nil
28	    elseif op == "matches" then
29	        return type(value) == "string" and value:match(lit) ~= nil
30	    end
31	    return false
32	end

36	local function shell_quote(s)
37	    -- wrap a string in single quotes, escaping any embedded single quotes
38	    return "'" .. tostring(s):gsub("'", "'\\''") .. "'"
39	end
40	-- }}}
41	
42	-- {{{ driver_path_for
43	local function driver_path_for(ref, drivers)
44	    -- binary callers (no extension mapping needed — they are executable directly)
45	    -- source callers: look up extension in driver table
46	    local ext = ref:match("%.([^./]+)$")
47	    if not ext then return ref end  -- no extension: treat as binary
48	    local driver = drivers["." .. ext]
49	    return driver
50	end
51	-- }}}
52	
53	-- {{{ run_task
54	-- The task boundary. In v1: synchronous. Future: hand this signature to
55	-- the thread pool. Returns outputs table and ok/err.
56	local function run_task(box, inputs, drivers, map_dir)
57	    local ref = box.ref
58	    local fn  = box.fn
59	
60	    -- locate the driver for this ref
61	    local driver = driver_path_for(ref, drivers)
62	    if not driver then
63	        return nil, "no driver found for ref '" .. ref .. "'"
64	    end
65	
66	    -- resolve ref relative to map_dir
67	    local abs_ref = map_dir .. "/" .. ref
68	
69	    -- build argument list: file fn arg_count [args...]
70	    local args = {}
71	    for _, port in ipairs(box.inputs or {}) do
72	        local val = inputs[port.name]
73	        if val == nil then
74	            -- nil input becomes JSON null
75	            args[#args + 1] = shell_quote(json.encode(nil))
76	        else
77	            args[#args + 1] = shell_quote(json.encode(val))
78	        end
79	    end
80	
81	    local cmd
82	    if fn then
83	        -- source caller: driver file fn arg_count [args...]
84	        cmd = driver .. " " .. shell_quote(abs_ref) .. " " ..

86	        for _, a in ipairs(args) do cmd = cmd .. " " .. a end
87	    else
88	        -- binary caller: run ref directly with args
89	        cmd = abs_ref .. " " .. #args
90	        for _, a in ipairs(args) do cmd = cmd .. " " .. a end
91	    end
92	
93	    -- invoke driver; capture stdout; errors go to stderr (visible in terminal)
94	    -- io.popen doesn't support stderr separation; redirect stderr to a tmp file
95	    local tmpfile = map_dir .. "/tmp/driver-stderr-" .. box.id .. ".txt"
96	    local full_cmd = "sh -c " .. shell_quote(cmd .. " 2>" .. tmpfile)
97	    local ph = io.popen(full_cmd, "r")
98	    local stdout = ph:read("*a")
99	    local ok_close = ph:close()

um... can you tell me about this "executor"?

## Assistant

`src/004-executor.lua` is the runtime engine for SoraMech — the thing that actually *runs* a map you've built in the editor.

When you invoke `soramech-runner.lua` on a map, the executor:

1. **Loads the graph** — reads all the box JSON files, the connections between them, and the driver table
2. **Pre-seeds the store** — a flat key-value table keyed by `"box_id.port_name"`. Literal values you set in the inspector get written here before anything runs
3. **Starts at the entry box** — whichever box is flagged as the entry point, it gets queued first
4. **Works through the queue** — for each box:
   - Checks whether all its input ports have values in the store (`inputs_satisfied`)
   - If yes: calls the appropriate language driver (lua, bash, etc.), gets back the output values
   - Pushes those outputs into the store under the target box's input keys
   - Enqueues any downstream boxes that are now fully satisfied
5. **Branch boxes** are special — they don't call a driver, they just look at their single input value and route it to the matching output port based on predicates (eq, contains, etc.)

So in terms of dataflow: the store is the "wire memory." A wired connection writes a value into `"to_box.to_port"` at runtime. A literal value pre-writes into `"box_id.port_name"` before the loop. If a wire later fires to the same key, it overwrites it — wires win.

The executor is currently synchronous (one box at a time, in queue order). The comment at the top of `run_task` marks it as the future thread pool swap point — when multiple boxes are independently ready, they'd be handed off to worker threads instead of run in sequence.

```
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Lua/LuaJIT language driver for SoraMech.
# Loads a .lua module, calls the named function with JSON-decoded arguments,
# and writes the return value(s) as a JSON array to stdout.
# Contract: <script> <file-path> <fn-name> <arg-count> [<arg> ...]

DIR="/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech"

FILE_PATH="${1}"
FN_NAME="${2}"
ARG_COUNT="${3}"
shift 3

if [ -z "${FILE_PATH}" ] || [ -z "${FN_NAME}" ]; then
    echo "lua.sh: missing file-path or fn-name" >&2
    exit 1
fi

if ! command -v luajit > /dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "lua.sh: luajit not found on PATH" >&2
    exit 1
fi

if [ ! -f "${FILE_PATH}" ]; then
    echo "lua.sh: file not found: ${FILE_PATH}" >&2
    exit 1
fi

# build a JSON array of the raw argument strings for passing into lua
ARGS_JSON="["
SEP=""
for ARG in "$@"; do
    ARGS_JSON="${ARGS_JSON}${SEP}${ARG}"
    SEP=","
done
ARGS_JSON="${ARGS_JSON}]"

# set LUA_PATH so dkjson is findable before the shim runs its first require
export LUA_PATH="${DIR}/libs/?.lua;${LUA_PATH:-;}"

# run luajit with the driver shim inline; the shim loads the module,
# decodes arguments, calls the function, and encodes the result
luajit - "${FILE_PATH}" "${FN_NAME}" "${ARGS_JSON}" << 'LUASHIM'
local json = require("dkjson")

-- read driver arguments
local file_path = arg[1]
local fn_name   = arg[2]
local args_raw  = arg[3]

-- add libs and the module's own directory to the path for any further requires
local script_dir = file_path:match("^(.+)/[^/]+$") or "."
package.path = script_dir .. "/?.lua;" ..
               "/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/libs/?.lua;" ..
               package.path

-- expose map root as a global so box functions can access data files without
-- needing it wired as an explicit input; convention: src/ is one level below root
SORAMECH_MAP_DIR = script_dir:match("^(.+)/[^/]+$") or script_dir

-- decode args array
local args_table, _, jerr = json.decode(args_raw)
if not args_table then
    io.stderr:write("lua.sh: failed to decode args JSON: " .. tostring(jerr) .. "\n")
    os.exit(1)
end

-- load the module
local ok, mod = pcall(dofile, file_path)
if not ok then
    io.stderr:write("lua.sh: error loading " .. file_path .. ": " .. tostring(mod) .. "\n")
    os.exit(1)
end

-- resolve the function
local fn = mod[fn_name]
if type(fn) ~= "function" then
    io.stderr:write("lua.sh: no public function '" .. fn_name .. "' in " .. file_path .. "\n")
    os.exit(1)
end

-- decode each arg from JSON to lua value; strings are passed as-is
local call_args = {}
for i, raw_arg in ipairs(args_table) do
    local val, _, aerr = json.decode(raw_arg)
    if aerr then
        -- not valid JSON — treat as a plain string
        call_args[i] = raw_arg
    else
        call_args[i] = val
    end
end

-- call the function; collect all return values into a table
local results = table.pack(fn(table.unpack(call_args)))

-- encode results as a JSON array (each return value is one element)
local encoded = {}
for i = 1, results.n do
    encoded[i] = json.encode(results[i])
end
print(json.encode(encoded))
LUASHIM
```

cp /mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/libs/ollama.lua maps/<your-map>/src/ollama.lua

Then in the editor: create a call box, hit browse, pick `ollama.lua`, choose `query` or `chat`. The file browser will auto-populate the ports:

- **`query`** — inputs: `prompt, model, host` → output: `result`
- **`chat`** — inputs: `prompt, system, model, host` → output: `result`

Wire `prompt` from an upstream box. Set `model`, `host`, and `system` as **literal port values** in the inspector (leave them blank to use the defaults: `llama3.2` + `localhost:11434`). That's it — no wrapper, no extra config.

If you want to call it from another Lua function instead: `local ollama = require("ollama")` works anywhere since `libs/` is already on the driver's package path.

18	    const mt = 1 - t;
19	    return {
20	      x: mt*mt*mt*x0 + 3*mt*mt*t*cx0 + 3*mt*t*t*cx1 + t*t*t*x1,
21	      y: mt*mt*mt*y0 + 3*mt*mt*t*cy0 + 3*mt*t*t*cy1 + t*t*t*y1,

27	  function draw_bezier(ctx, x0, y0, x1, y1, color, selected) {
28	    const cx0 = x0 + BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET;
29	    const cy0 = y0;
30	    const cx1 = x1 - BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET;
31	    const cy1 = y1;
32	
33	    ctx.beginPath();
34	    ctx.moveTo(x0, y0);
35	    ctx.bezierCurveTo(cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1, x1, y1);

37	    ctx.lineWidth   = selected ? 2.5 : 1.5;
38	    ctx.setLineDash(selected ? [] : []);
39	    ctx.stroke();

44	  // Returns the connection record if cursor (wx,wy) is within HIT_THRESHOLD pixels
45	  // of any wire, or null. Operates in world space; threshold is adjusted for zoom.
46	  function hit_test_wire(wx, wy, zoom) {
47	    const threshold = HIT_THRESHOLD_PX / zoom;
48	    for (const id in Boxes.boxes) {
49	      const box = Boxes.boxes[id];
50	      for (const c of box.connections || []) {
51	        if (c.from_box !== id) continue;

53	        const src  = Boxes.get_port_world_pos(c.from_box, c.from_output || c.from_port, 'output');

57	        const cx0 = src.x + BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET, cy0 = src.y;
58	        const cx1 = dst.x - BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET, cy1 = dst.y;
59	
60	        for (let i = 0; i <= HIT_SAMPLES; i++) {

63	          const dx = wx - p.x, dy = wy - p.y;

13	  async function request(method, path, body) {
14	    const opts = { method, headers: {} };
15	    if (body !== undefined) {
16	      opts.headers['Content-Type'] = 'application/json';
17	      opts.body = JSON.stringify(body);
18	    }
19	    const res = await fetch(base_url + path, opts);
20	    const text = await res.text();
21	    let data;
22	    try { data = JSON.parse(text); } catch { data = { error: text }; }
23	    if (!res.ok) throw new Error(data.error || `HTTP ${res.status}`);
24	    return data;
25	  }
