83	-- {{{ safe_path
84	-- Returns the resolved path if it is within root, nil otherwise.
85	-- Prevents path traversal outside the maps root.
86	local function safe_path(root, ...)
87	    local parts = { root }
88	    for _, p in ipairs({...}) do
89	        -- reject any component containing ".."
90	        if tostring(p):find("%.%.", 1, true) then return nil end
91	        parts[#parts + 1] = p
92	    end
93	    return table.concat(parts, "/")
94	end

can you tell me how this is implemented in comparison to the previous one?

## Assistant

The `ollama.lua` call box and the `llm-route` box solve different problems, and the executor treats them very differently.

**`call` box using `ollama.lua`:**

The executor doesn't know or care that it's talking to Ollama. It just sees a box with `kind: "call"`, looks up the driver (`drivers/lua.sh`), spawns a subprocess, passes the inputs as JSON arguments, and collects the return values as named output ports. The Lua function does the HTTP call internally. The executor's job is done after the function returns — it pushes the output values into the store and enqueues whatever downstream boxes are now satisfied.

**`llm-route` box:**

The executor handles it directly in `fire_connections` without spawning any subprocess. There's no driver involved. Instead, when the box runs, `llm_route_call()` in the executor itself calls `require("ollama")` and makes the Ollama request inline in Lua. The result isn't an output value pushed to downstream ports — it's a **routing decision**. The executor looks at which output port name matches the LLM's reply, then fires only that port's wire, passing the original `text` input through as the value. The other ports stay silent.

So the key difference is **data flow vs. control flow**:

- `call` box: produces output values, all wired outputs fire, downstream boxes receive data
- `llm-route` box: produces no data of its own, exactly one output wire fires, that wire carries the input text through — the box is a switch, not a transformer

It's the same distinction as between a `call` box and a `branch` box. In fact `llm-route` is implemented almost identically to `branch` in the executor — the only difference is that `branch` evaluates a hard-coded predicate (eq, contains, etc.) while `llm-route` asks Ollama to make the judgment call.

It's hard-coded into all three system layers — which is the same situation as `branch`.

**In the inspector** (`004-inspector.js`): the kind dropdown lists it by name, and `show()` has a dedicated block that renders its specific UI. Touch the inspector to change what appears in the panel.

**In the wires** (`006-wires.js`): two more explicit checks — one to use `from_port` instead of `from_output` when drawing a wire from it, one for the colored-wire logic.

So it's spread across three files and none of it is swappable. Compare that to `ollama.lua`, which is entirely standalone — delete the file and nothing in the system breaks.

The root issue is that **control flow can't live in a user function**. A `call` box always fires all its output wires after the function returns — the executor doesn't give the function any way to say "only fire this one port." So any box that needs to choose between output paths has to be a special kind that the executor knows about, which means it ends up baked in.

One way to fix this: give functions a way to return a *route signal* — a special return value that the executor interprets as "fire this named port with this value." Then `llm-route` could just be a Lua function in `libs/` that returns `{ _route = "positive", _value = text }`, and the executor would handle it generically for any call box that returns that shape. No new kind, no changes to the inspector or wires.

## Assistant

The second example is the revealing one. Let me think through it:

```
function do_other_thing(d, e, f)
  if d == 2 then return (e, f)
  else return d
```

The system has to know: when two values come back, do they go to ports 1 and 2 positionally? Or to specific named ports? And when one value comes back, which port does it go to — the first one, or the one named `d`?

The current executor maps returns strictly by position: first return value → first output port, second → second, and so on. So there's actually a partial solution already baked in — if a function returns `nil` for a position, the executor skips writing that key to the store, which means the downstream box never becomes satisfied and its wire effectively doesn't fire. But to use it you'd have to write `return nil, e, f` or `return d, nil, nil` and know the port order by heart, which is fragile.

The cleaner fix is a thin routing wrapper in the standard library:

```lua
local sora = require("soramech")

function do_other_thing(d, e, f)
  if d == 2 then return sora.routes({ e_out = e, f_out = f })
  else return sora.route("d_out", d)
end
```

`sora.route` and `sora.routes` just return a specially-marked table — `{ __soramech_route = true, port = "d_out", value = d }`. The driver detects that marker and outputs a different JSON shape. The executor detects it in `fire_connections` and fires only the named ports instead of doing positional mapping.

The payoff: `llm-route` becomes a regular Lua function in `libs/` that calls Ollama and returns `sora.route(matched_port, text)`. The special kind disappears from the executor, inspector, and wires entirely — replaced by a library call any function can use.

The mutation case isn't really a system problem — it's a code readability problem. The port is correctly named `b`; the user just has to read their own function to know `b` might be 2. The parsing approach is sound. But it still doesn't tell the executor which return value maps to which port at runtime. `return a, b, c` gives the driver three anonymous positional values — the names are lost.

The way to fix both problems at once: **require functions to return a table with named keys.**

```lua
function blahblahblah(a, b, c)
  if a == 2 then b = 2 end
  return { a = a, b = b, c = c }
end

function classify(text)
  if score > 0.5 then return { positive = text }
  else                return { negative = text }
  end
end
```

The parser reads table keys from return statements — that's already closer to what it does. The driver detects that the return value is a table (not an array) and uses it as a named map. The executor fires only the ports whose keys appear in the returned table. No positional mapping, no library wrapper, no fragility.

The mutation case looks like `{ b = b }` — the key is the port name, the value is whatever b is at that moment. That's honest and accurate.

Constants come out naturally too: `return { version = "1.0" }` — the inspector sees `"1.0"` and marks the port as constant. Complex expressions: `return { result = foo(x) }` — shows `foo(x)` verbatim, which is ugly but accurate.

The one cost: functions need to always return a table. That's a convention the user has to follow, but it's a small one and it buys complete decoupling from positional ordering.

The one case where disambiguation works automatically: when different return paths produce **different numbers of values**. If path 1 returns 3 values and path 2 returns 1, the driver can check the count and know which path fired. That covers a lot of practical cases:

  if a == 2 then return a, b, c   -- 3 values → path 1
  else return c                   -- 1 value  → path 2
end

But your example — two paths both returning 3 values — is unresolvable without some form of tagging. The driver genuinely cannot tell them apart.

So the honest picture is:

- Different value counts → works, no idiom needed, just count-based disambiguation
- Same value counts, different variable order → unsolvable at runtime without tagging

For that second case, you're stuck choosing between: source instrumentation (fragile, complex), a tagging convention, or accepting that port names are approximate/informational when paths have equal lengths.

Maybe the pragmatic answer is: implement count-based disambiguation, show the multi-section display in the inspector, and document that "same-length ambiguous paths" are a known limitation. In practice most routing functions return different amounts from different paths anyway.

1	# 208 — Port literal values
2	
3	## Status
4	
5	open
6	
7	## Current behavior
8	
9	Input ports on a box can only receive values from wired connections. If no wire
10	is connected to an input port, the box never becomes "ready" and the runner
11	skips it silently. There is no way to supply a constant value to an input from
12	the editor.
13	
14	## Intended behavior
15	
16	Each input port has an optional literal value editable directly in the inspector
17	panel. If the value field is non-empty and no wire is connected to that port,
18	the literal value is used at runtime. If a wire IS connected, the wire's value
19	overwrites the literal at runtime — wires always win.
20	
21	The value field accepts JSON notation (numbers, strings with quotes, arrays,
22	objects) or bare strings for convenience. The executor tries JSON.decode first;
23	if that fails, the raw string is used.
24	
25	This lets a box be fully configured from the inspector without needing a
26	dedicated "data" box wired to it for every constant.
27	
28	## Suggested implementation steps
29	
30	1. **Data model** — port objects in `box.inputs` gain an optional `value`
31	   string field. No schema change needed (extra fields are already tolerated).
32

34	   `show_value` boolean parameter. When true, each port row shows a second
35	   text input for the value alongside the name input. On change, updates
36	   `ports[i].value` and calls `save()`.
37	
38	3. **Executor (`004-executor.lua`)** — add a pre-seeding pass before the main
39	   execution loop: for every box, for every input port that has a non-empty
40	   `value`, write `store[box_id.."."..port.name]` with the parsed value.
41	   Existing `inputs_satisfied` and `collect_inputs` logic requires no changes

1	# 210 — LLM route box
2	
3	## Status
4	
5	open
6	
7	## Current behavior
8	
9	Routing between box paths is done by the `branch` box kind using hard-coded
10	predicates (eq, contains, matches). There is no way to use an LLM judgment
11	to decide which path to take.
12	
13	## Intended behavior
14	
15	A new box kind `llm-route` combines an Ollama call and a routing decision in
16	one box. It takes text input (typically from a previous LLM call), a prompt
17	instruction describing the routing criteria, and produces N named output ports
18	— one per possible route. The LLM is asked to reply with exactly one of the
19	port names; that port's wire fires and passes the original text through.
20	
21	Inspector UI mirrors the branch box: manually-configurable inputs (with literal
22	value fields), and a user-addable output port list (just names — no predicates).
23	
24	Default inputs on a fresh llm-route box: `text`, `prompt`, `model`, `host`.
25	The text value flows through whichever port is selected by the LLM. An `else`
26	port can be added to catch unmatched replies.
27	
28	Connections from this box use `from_port` (same as branch) so wires render
29	with per-port palette colors.
30	
31	## Suggested implementation steps

37	3. `004-executor.lua` — add `llm_route_call()` helper (require ollama, compose
38	   routing prompt, match reply to port name); handle in execute loop and
39	   fire_connections.
40	
41	## Related documents

43	- assets/js/006-wires.js — create_connection, draw_all

1	# 108 — Branch box and predicate routing
2	
3	## Status
4	
5	complete
6	
7	## Implementation notes
8	
9	Schema fix (2026-05-07): `src/001-schema.lua` `validate_box` previously applied
10	kind-specific connection validation to ALL connections in a box file. Since both
11	endpoint boxes store the identical 4-field connection record, a call box file
12	can contain a reciprocal record with `from_port` (from a branch box), and a
13	branch box file can contain a reciprocal record with `from_output` (from a call
14	box). Fixed by routing connection validation on `c.from_box == box.id`:
15	outgoing records get the strict kind-specific validator; incoming reciprocal
16	records get a looser validator (`validate_incoming_connection`) that accepts
17	either `from_output` or `from_port`.
18	
19	Executor retry: branch inputs are cleared from the store on retry so the
20	upstream box re-fires cleanly. `retry_vary` fields are pseudo-randomized in
21	the range before the upstream box is re-queued.
22	
23	Verified with `maps/branch-test/`: n=30 routes to write-low (result="30"),
24	n=70 routes to write-high (result="70"). hello and driver-test maps pass
25	regression.
26	
27	## Blockers
28	
29	- 104 (executor must exist; predicate evaluation is added to it here)
30	- 107 (wire UI must exist; branch port wires need visual distinction)
31	
32	## Current behavior
33	
34	No branch box type exists. All routing is linear (one output, one wire).
35	There is no mechanism to conditionally fire one box vs. another based on
36	a value.
37	
38	## Intended behavior
39	
40	A box with "kind": "branch" has named output ports instead of positional
41	outputs. Each port (except "else") carries a predicate. The executor
42	evaluates each predicate against the input value and fires the first port
43	whose predicate is true. If no predicate matches, the "else" port fires.
44	
45	Port predicates:
46	  { "op": "eq"|"lt"|"gt"|"lte"|"gte"|"contains"|"matches", "value": <literal> }
47	
48	"matches" uses lua pattern matching against a string input.
49	"contains" checks whether a string input contains the literal as a substring.
50	
51	Branch box behavior in two scenarios:
52	
53	  Numeric/string predicate branch:
54	    - "else" port must be wired to something, or the runner refuses to start
55	    - If no predicate matches and "else" fires, the program continues from
56	      the "else" port's connection
57	    - The runner logs which port fired for each branch box in last-run.json
58	
59	  LLM output classification:
60	    - "else" port may be left unwired in the box file (omit the connection)
61	    - If "else" fires on an unwired port, the runner re-queues the box
62	      whose output fed this branch box (the immediately upstream box in the
63	      path that led here)
64	    - The upstream box is re-invoked with any fields marked "retry_vary"
65	      in its box file pseudo-randomized (e.g., temperature, top_p)
66	    - Retry limit: configurable per branch box, default 3. Exceeded retry
67	      limit is a hard error, not a silent loop.
68	
69	The "retry_vary" field on a box:
70	  "retry_vary": { "temperature": { "min": 0.5, "max": 1.2 } }
71	  On retry, any field listed here is replaced with a random value in range.
72	
73	## Suggested implementation steps
74	
75	1. Add branch box handling to src/004-executor.lua:
76	   - When a branch box fires, evaluate each port's predicate in order.
77	   - Fire the first matching port's connection.
78	   - If no match, fire "else". If "else" has no connection, trigger retry.
79	2. Add retry state to the executor: a per-box retry counter in run state.
80	   On retry trigger, decrement counter; if zero, halt with error.
81	3. Implement retry_vary: before re-invoking the upstream box, for each
82	   field in its retry_vary table, generate a random value in the given
83	   range and override the corresponding input before invocation.
84	4. Update src/003-loader.lua validation: branch boxes without an "else"
85	   connection are valid only if at least one upstream box has a retry_vary
86	   field. If neither condition is met, the validator rejects the map.
87	5. Update assets/js/006-wires.js: branch box ports each get a distinct
88	   color from a cycling palette. Port labels ("rogue", "wizard", "else")
89	   are drawn alongside the port dot on the canvas.
90	6. Update assets/js/004-inspector.js: branch box inspector shows port list
91	   with add/remove, predicate editor per port (op dropdown + value input),
92	   and retry limit input.
93	
94	## Related documents
95	
96	- docs/001-architecture.md — branch box file format
97	- issues/104 — executor (predicate evaluation added here)
98	- issues/107 — wire UI (port color and label rendering)
99	
100	## Notes
101	
102	Port predicate evaluation order matters: ports are evaluated in the order
103	they appear in the "ports" array. The first matching predicate fires. This
104	is intentional — the user controls priority by ordering ports. Document
105	this in the inspector UI (e.g., "ports are evaluated top to bottom").
106	
107	The retry mechanism is deliberately minimal: it re-runs one upstream box
108	with varied parameters. It is not a general loop construct. If the user
109	needs complex retry logic (backoff, multiple retries with different
110	strategies), they should model it with explicit boxes wired in a cycle
111	through a branch box. The built-in retry is for the common case of
112	"ask the LLM again with a different temperature."
113	
```

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:

The session continued work on SoraMech — a visual FSM/dataflow editor. Over the course of the conversation, the user's requests evolved significantly:

**Immediate fixes (session start):**

**Bug fixes:**

- "M as the output" — parser picked up module-level `return M` and a comma-in-string bug

**Features added:**
- Renameable port names in inspector

- `llm-route` box kind (later reconsidered for removal)

**Major architectural direction (final discussion):**
- Remove named output ports entirely
- Every box has ONE output wire carrying the raw return value
- Optional comparator on that wire splits into lt/eq/gt wires; errors if value is not a strict number
- Input port names are read-only, derived from parsing the function's parameter names (no renaming)
- `branch` and `llm-route` box kinds go away — replaced by comparator mechanism on any box
- Connection model changes to `{from_box, from_branch (null/"lt"/"eq"/"gt"), to_box, to_input}`
- **"Instead of making new issue files, update the old ones"** — user explicitly said to update existing issue files to reflect this new direction

2. Key Technical Concepts:

- SoraMech: visual FSM/dataflow editor, three components (server, runner, browser editor)
- Box kinds: `call`, `branch`, `data`, `llm-route` (last two being removed/reconsidered)
- Connection schema: `{from_box, from_output|from_port, to_box, to_input}` → evolving to `{from_box, from_branch, to_box, to_input}`
- Executor store: flat key-value table `"box_id.port_name" → value`; literal port values pre-seeded before loop; wires overwrite
- Language drivers: lua.sh, bash.sh, c.sh — called via subprocess, return JSON arrays
- File browser: parses Lua `function M.name(args)` pattern; uses `lua_fn_end()` nesting tracker to bound function bodies
- Ollama integration: `libs/ollama.lua` using `curl` via `io.popen`; `M.query` and `M.chat`
- Extra src dirs: stored in `meta.json.extra_src_dirs`; served via new `/maps/:name/extrasrc` routes
- New comparator design: one output wire per box; optional compare button → lt/eq/gt branches; non-number → error

3. Files and Code Sections:

  - ref field changed to editable text input

  - `parse_lua_returns()` — added check: `/["']|\.\./.test(expr)` → treat whole expression as complex (adds "result")

4. Errors and Fixes:

- **"no public functions found"**: `API.get_src_file()` used shared `request()` which does `JSON.parse(text)`. Lua source isn't valid JSON → parse fails → returns `{error: <raw text>}` object. Fixed by making `get_src_file` do raw `fetch().text()` bypassing the helper.

- **"M" as output**: Two bugs: (1) function body slice for last function extended past the function's closing `end` to the module-level `return M`. Fixed with `lua_fn_end()` nesting tracker. (2) `"Hello, "` string literal split by comma gave two parts, neither bare identifier, and since `parts.length !== 1` the "result" fallback didn't apply. - **stale comment**: After changing ref field to editable, left an old comment `// ref: read-only display + browse button`. Updated to `// ref: editable text input + browse button to populate via file browser`.

1	# 210 — LLM route box
2	
3	## Status
4	
5	open
6	
7	## Current behavior
8	
9	Routing between box paths is done by the `branch` box kind using hard-coded
10	predicates (eq, contains, matches). There is no way to use an LLM judgment
11	to decide which path to take.
12	
13	## Intended behavior
14	
15	A new box kind `llm-route` combines an Ollama call and a routing decision in
16	one box. It takes text input (typically from a previous LLM call), a prompt
17	instruction describing the routing criteria, and produces N named output ports
18	— one per possible route. The LLM is asked to reply with exactly one of the
19	port names; that port's wire fires and passes the original text through.
20	
21	Inspector UI mirrors the branch box: manually-configurable inputs (with literal
22	value fields), and a user-addable output port list (just names — no predicates).
23	
24	Default inputs on a fresh llm-route box: `text`, `prompt`, `model`, `host`.
25	The text value flows through whichever port is selected by the LLM. An `else`
26	port can be added to catch unmatched replies.
27	
28	Connections from this box use `from_port` (same as branch) so wires render
29	with per-port palette colors.
30	
31	## Suggested implementation steps
32	
33	1. `006-wires.js` — add `llm-route` to the `from_port` branch in
34	   `create_connection`, and to the colored-wire logic in `draw_all`.
35	2. `004-inspector.js` — add `'llm-route'` to kind dropdown; add
36	   `mk_simple_port_list()` (addable port rows, name only); add show() section.
37	3. `004-executor.lua` — add `llm_route_call()` helper (require ollama, compose
38	   routing prompt, match reply to port name); handle in execute loop and
39	   fire_connections.
40	
41	## Related documents
42	
43	- assets/js/006-wires.js — create_connection, draw_all
44	- assets/js/004-inspector.js — show, mk_branch_port_list
45	- src/004-executor.lua — execute, fire_connections
46	- libs/ollama.lua — M.query used for routing call
47	
```

1	# 208 — Port literal values
2	
3	## Status
4	
5	open
6	
7	## Current behavior
8	
9	Input ports on a box can only receive values from wired connections. If no wire
10	is connected to an input port, the box never becomes "ready" and the runner
11	skips it silently. There is no way to supply a constant value to an input from
12	the editor.
13	
14	## Intended behavior
15	
16	Each input port has an optional literal value editable directly in the inspector
17	panel. If the value field is non-empty and no wire is connected to that port,
18	the literal value is used at runtime. If a wire IS connected, the wire's value
19	overwrites the literal at runtime — wires always win.
20	
21	The value field accepts JSON notation (numbers, strings with quotes, arrays,
22	objects) or bare strings for convenience. The executor tries JSON.decode first;
23	if that fails, the raw string is used.
24	
25	This lets a box be fully configured from the inspector without needing a
26	dedicated "data" box wired to it for every constant.
27	
28	## Suggested implementation steps
29	
30	1. **Data model** — port objects in `box.inputs` gain an optional `value`
31	   string field. No schema change needed (extra fields are already tolerated).
32	
33	2. **Inspector (`004-inspector.js`)** — `mk_port_display()` gains a
34	   `show_value` boolean parameter. When true, each port row shows a second
35	   text input for the value alongside the name input. On change, updates
36	   `ports[i].value` and calls `save()`.
37	
38	3. **Executor (`004-executor.lua`)** — add a pre-seeding pass before the main
39	   execution loop: for every box, for every input port that has a non-empty
40	   `value`, write `store[box_id.."."..port.name]` with the parsed value.
41	   Existing `inputs_satisfied` and `collect_inputs` logic requires no changes
42	   because they only read from the store.
43	
44	## Related documents
45	
46	- assets/js/004-inspector.js — mk_port_display, show
47	- src/004-executor.lua — execute, inputs_satisfied
48	- docs/001-architecture.md — store model, box.inputs schema
49	
```

1	# 108 — Branch box and predicate routing
2	
3	## Status
4	
5	complete
6	
7	## Implementation notes
8	
9	Schema fix (2026-05-07): `src/001-schema.lua` `validate_box` previously applied
10	kind-specific connection validation to ALL connections in a box file. Since both
11	endpoint boxes store the identical 4-field connection record, a call box file
12	can contain a reciprocal record with `from_port` (from a branch box), and a
13	branch box file can contain a reciprocal record with `from_output` (from a call
14	box). Fixed by routing connection validation on `c.from_box == box.id`:
15	outgoing records get the strict kind-specific validator; incoming reciprocal
16	records get a looser validator (`validate_incoming_connection`) that accepts
17	either `from_output` or `from_port`.
18	
19	Executor retry: branch inputs are cleared from the store on retry so the
20	upstream box re-fires cleanly. `retry_vary` fields are pseudo-randomized in
21	the range before the upstream box is re-queued.
22	
23	Verified with `maps/branch-test/`: n=30 routes to write-low (result="30"),
24	n=70 routes to write-high (result="70"). hello and driver-test maps pass
25	regression.
26	
27	## Blockers
28	
29	- 104 (executor must exist; predicate evaluation is added to it here)
30	- 107 (wire UI must exist; branch port wires need visual distinction)
31	
32	## Current behavior
33	
34	No branch box type exists. All routing is linear (one output, one wire).
35	There is no mechanism to conditionally fire one box vs. another based on
36	a value.
37	
38	## Intended behavior
39	
40	A box with "kind": "branch" has named output ports instead of positional
41	outputs. Each port (except "else") carries a predicate. The executor
42	evaluates each predicate against the input value and fires the first port
43	whose predicate is true. If no predicate matches, the "else" port fires.
44	
45	Port predicates:
46	  { "op": "eq"|"lt"|"gt"|"lte"|"gte"|"contains"|"matches", "value": <literal> }
47	
48	"matches" uses lua pattern matching against a string input.
49	"contains" checks whether a string input contains the literal as a substring.
50	
51	Branch box behavior in two scenarios:
52	
53	  Numeric/string predicate branch:
54	    - "else" port must be wired to something, or the runner refuses to start
55	    - If no predicate matches and "else" fires, the program continues from
56	      the "else" port's connection
57	    - The runner logs which port fired for each branch box in last-run.json
58	
59	  LLM output classification:
60	    - "else" port may be left unwired in the box file (omit the connection)
61	    - If "else" fires on an unwired port, the runner re-queues the box
62	      whose output fed this branch box (the immediately upstream box in the
63	      path that led here)
64	    - The upstream box is re-invoked with any fields marked "retry_vary"
65	      in its box file pseudo-randomized (e.g., temperature, top_p)
66	    - Retry limit: configurable per branch box, default 3. Exceeded retry
67	      limit is a hard error, not a silent loop.
68	
69	The "retry_vary" field on a box:
70	  "retry_vary": { "temperature": { "min": 0.5, "max": 1.2 } }
71	  On retry, any field listed here is replaced with a random value in range.
72	
73	## Suggested implementation steps
74	
75	1. Add branch box handling to src/004-executor.lua:
76	   - When a branch box fires, evaluate each port's predicate in order.
77	   - Fire the first matching port's connection.
78	   - If no match, fire "else". If "else" has no connection, trigger retry.
79	2. Add retry state to the executor: a per-box retry counter in run state.
80	   On retry trigger, decrement counter; if zero, halt with error.
81	3. Implement retry_vary: before re-invoking the upstream box, for each
82	   field in its retry_vary table, generate a random value in the given
83	   range and override the corresponding input before invocation.
84	4. Update src/003-loader.lua validation: branch boxes without an "else"
85	   connection are valid only if at least one upstream box has a retry_vary
86	   field. If neither condition is met, the validator rejects the map.
87	5. Update assets/js/006-wires.js: branch box ports each get a distinct
88	   color from a cycling palette. Port labels ("rogue", "wizard", "else")
89	   are drawn alongside the port dot on the canvas.
90	6. Update assets/js/004-inspector.js: branch box inspector shows port list
91	   with add/remove, predicate editor per port (op dropdown + value input),
92	   and retry limit input.
93	
94	## Related documents
95	
96	- docs/001-architecture.md — branch box file format
97	- issues/104 — executor (predicate evaluation added here)
98	- issues/107 — wire UI (port color and label rendering)
99	
100	## Notes
101	
102	Port predicate evaluation order matters: ports are evaluated in the order
103	they appear in the "ports" array. The first matching predicate fires. This
104	is intentional — the user controls priority by ordering ports. Document
105	this in the inspector UI (e.g., "ports are evaluated top to bottom").
106	
107	The retry mechanism is deliberately minimal: it re-runs one upstream box
108	with varied parameters. It is not a general loop construct. If the user
109	needs complex retry logic (backoff, multiple retries with different
110	strategies), they should model it with explicit boxes wired in a cycle
111	through a branch box. The built-in retry is for the common case of
112	"ask the LLM again with a different temperature."
113	
```

1	-- Schema definitions and validators for SoraMech map files.
2	-- Validates box files, meta.json, and drivers.json against the spec
3	-- in docs/001-architecture.md. Used by both the runner and the server
4	-- before any file is written to disk.
5	
6	local DIR = "/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech"
7	
8	local M = {}
9	
10	-- {{{ valid_kinds
11	local valid_kinds = { call = true, branch = true, data = true }
12	-- }}}
13	
14	-- {{{ valid_ops
15	local valid_ops = {
16	    eq = true, lt = true, gt = true,
17	    lte = true, gte = true, contains = true, matches = true
18	}
19	-- }}}
20	
21	-- {{{ err
22	local function err(errors, msg)
23	    errors[#errors + 1] = msg
24	end
25	-- }}}
26	
27	-- {{{ validate_port
28	local function validate_port(port, i, errors)
29	    -- "else" port has no predicate; all others require one
30	    if port.name == nil then
31	        err(errors, "port[" .. i .. "] missing 'name'")
32	        return
33	    end
34	    if port.name == "else" then return end
35	    if port.predicate == nil then
36	        err(errors, "port '" .. port.name .. "' missing predicate (only 'else' may omit it)")
37	        return
38	    end
39	    local p = port.predicate
40	    if p.op == nil then
41	        err(errors, "port '" .. port.name .. "' predicate missing 'op'")
42	    elseif not valid_ops[p.op] then
43	        err(errors, "port '" .. port.name .. "' predicate has unknown op '" .. p.op .. "'")
44	    end
45	    if p.value == nil then
46	        err(errors, "port '" .. port.name .. "' predicate missing 'value'")
47	    end
48	end
49	-- }}}
50	
51	-- {{{ validate_connection
52	local function validate_connection(c, i, errors)
53	    -- all four fields must be present; both endpoint files store the same record
54	    if c.from_box == nil then
55	        err(errors, "connection[" .. i .. "] missing 'from_box'")
56	    end
57	    if c.from_output == nil then
58	        err(errors, "connection[" .. i .. "] missing 'from_output'")
59	    end
60	    if c.to_box == nil then
61	        err(errors, "connection[" .. i .. "] missing 'to_box'")
62	    end
63	    if c.to_input == nil then
64	        err(errors, "connection[" .. i .. "] missing 'to_input'")
65	    end
66	end
67	-- }}}
68	
69	-- {{{ validate_port_connection
70	local function validate_port_connection(c, i, errors)
71	    -- branch box connections use from_port instead of from_output;
72	    -- from_box and to_box/to_input are still required for the reciprocal check
73	    if c.from_box == nil then
74	        err(errors, "branch connection[" .. i .. "] missing 'from_box'")
75	    end
76	    if c.from_port == nil then
77	        err(errors, "branch connection[" .. i .. "] missing 'from_port'")
78	    end
79	    if c.to_box == nil then
80	        err(errors, "branch connection[" .. i .. "] missing 'to_box'")
81	    end

84	    end
85	end
86	-- }}}
87	
88	-- {{{ validate_incoming_connection
89	local function validate_incoming_connection(c, i, errors)
90	    -- reciprocal record written to the receiving end; accept either from_output or from_port
91	    -- since we don't know the source box kind here
92	    if c.from_box == nil then
93	        err(errors, "connection[" .. i .. "] missing 'from_box'")
94	    end
95	    if c.from_output == nil and c.from_port == nil then
96	        err(errors, "connection[" .. i .. "] missing both 'from_output' and 'from_port'")
97	    end
98	    if c.to_box == nil then
99	        err(errors, "connection[" .. i .. "] missing 'to_box'")
100	    end

102	        err(errors, "connection[" .. i .. "] missing 'to_input'")
103	    end
104	end
105	-- }}}
106	
107	-- {{{ validate_box
108	function M.validate_box(box)
109	    local errors = {}
110	    if type(box) ~= "table" then
111	        return { "box must be a table" }
112	    end
113	
114	    if box.id == nil or type(box.id) ~= "string" then
115	        err(errors, "missing or non-string 'id'")
116	    end
117	    if box.label == nil or type(box.label) ~= "string" then
118	        err(errors, "missing or non-string 'label'")
119	    end
120	    if box.kind == nil or not valid_kinds[box.kind] then
121	        err(errors, "missing or invalid 'kind' (must be call, branch, or data)")
122	    end
123	
124	    if box.kind == "call" then
125	        if box.ref == nil or type(box.ref) ~= "string" then
126	            err(errors, "call box missing 'ref'")
127	        end
128	        -- fn is optional for binary callers (shell scripts, binaries)
129	        if box.inputs ~= nil and type(box.inputs) ~= "table" then
130	            err(errors, "'inputs' must be an array")
131	        end
132	        if box.outputs ~= nil and type(box.outputs) ~= "table" then
133	            err(errors, "'outputs' must be an array")
134	        end
135	        if box.connections ~= nil then
136	            for i, c in ipairs(box.connections) do
137	                -- outgoing connections (this box is the source) require from_output;
138	                -- incoming reciprocal records accept either from_output or from_port
139	                if c.from_box == box.id then
140	                    validate_connection(c, i, errors)
141	                else

145	        end
146

148	        if box.inputs == nil or type(box.inputs) ~= "table" then
149	            err(errors, "branch box missing 'inputs'")
150	        end
151	        if box.ports == nil or type(box.ports) ~= "table" or #box.ports == 0 then
152	            err(errors, "branch box missing 'ports' array")
153	        else
154	            local has_else = false
155	            for i, p in ipairs(box.ports) do
156	                validate_port(p, i, errors)
157	                if p.name == "else" then has_else = true end
158	            end
159	            if not has_else then
160	                err(errors, "branch box missing required 'else' port")
161	            end
162	        end
163	        if box.retry_limit ~= nil and type(box.retry_limit) ~= "number" then
164	            err(errors, "'retry_limit' must be a number")
165	        end
166	        if box.connections ~= nil then
167	            for i, c in ipairs(box.connections) do
168	                -- outgoing connections (this box is the source) require from_port;
169	                -- incoming reciprocal records from call boxes use from_output
170	                if c.from_box == box.id then
171	                    validate_port_connection(c, i, errors)
172	                else
173	                    validate_incoming_connection(c, i, errors)
174	                end
175	            end

1	-- Graph loader for the SoraMech runner.
2	-- Reads a map directory into an in-memory graph structure and validates
3	-- its integrity before execution begins. The graph is the runner's
4	-- internal representation of the map.
5	
6	local DIR = "/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech"
7	
8	package.path = DIR .. "/libs/?.lua;" ..
9	               DIR .. "/src/?.lua;" ..
10	               package.path
11	
12	local json   = require("dkjson")
13	local schema = require("001-schema")
14	
15	local M = {}
16	
17	-- {{{ read_json_file
18	local function read_json_file(path)
19	    local f, ferr = io.open(path, "r")
20	    if not f then return nil, "cannot open " .. path .. ": " .. ferr end
21	    local raw = f:read("*a")
22	    f:close()
23	    local obj, _, jerr = json.decode(raw)
24	    if not obj then return nil, "JSON error in " .. path .. ": " .. tostring(jerr) end
25	    return obj
26	end
27	-- }}}
28	
29	-- {{{ load_drivers
30	local function load_drivers(map_dir)
31	    -- map-local drivers take priority; fall back to global drivers alongside the runner
32	    local local_path  = map_dir .. "/drivers.json"
33	    local global_path = DIR .. "/drivers.json"
34	
35	    local drivers = {}
36	
37	    -- load global first so local can override
38	    local gd, _ = read_json_file(global_path)
39	    if gd then
40	        for ext, path in pairs(gd) do drivers[ext] = path end
41	    end
42	
43	    local ld, _ = read_json_file(local_path)
44	    if ld then
45	        for ext, path in pairs(ld) do drivers[ext] = path end
46	    end
47	
48	    return drivers
49	end
50	-- }}}
51	
52	-- {{{ list_box_files
53	local function list_box_files(boxes_dir)
54	    local files = {}
55	    local handle = io.popen("ls " .. boxes_dir .. "/*.json 2>/dev/null")
56	    if not handle then return files end
57	    local listing = handle:read("*a")
58	    handle:close()
59	    for path in listing:gmatch("[^\n]+") do
60	        files[#files + 1] = path
61	    end
62	    return files
63	end
64	-- }}}
65	
66	-- {{{ extension_of
67	local function extension_of(path)
68	    return path:match("%.([^./]+)$") and ("." .. path:match("%.([^./]+)$")) or ""
69	end
70	-- }}}
71	
72	-- {{{ detect_cycles
73	-- depth-first search from start_id; branch boxes are cycle-break nodes
74	-- (a cycle that passes through a branch box is the retry pattern — allowed)
75	local function detect_cycles(boxes, start_id)
76	    local visited  = {}
77	    local in_stack = {}
78	    local errors   = {}
79	
80	    local function dfs(id)
81	        if in_stack[id] then
82	            errors[#errors + 1] = "cycle detected at box '" .. id ..
83	                "' that does not pass through a branch box"
84	            return
85	        end
86	        if visited[id] then return end
87	        visited[id]  = true
88	        in_stack[id] = true
89	
90	        local box = boxes[id]
91	        if not box then
92	            in_stack[id] = false
93	            return
94	        end
95	
96	        -- branch boxes break cycles — stop DFS at them (retry loops are valid)
97	        if box.kind == "branch" then
98	            in_stack[id] = false
99	            return
100	        end
101	
102	        for _, c in ipairs(box.connections or {}) do
103	            if c.from_box == id then
104	                dfs(c.to_box)
105	            end
106	        end
107	
108	        in_stack[id] = false
109	    end
110	
111	    dfs(start_id)
112	    return errors
113	end
114	-- }}}
115	
116	-- {{{ load_map
117	function M.load_map(map_dir)
118	    local errors = {}
119	
120	    -- meta.json
121	    local meta, merr = read_json_file(map_dir .. "/meta.json")
122	    if not meta then
123	        return nil, { merr }
124	    end
125	    local meta_errs = schema.validate_meta(meta)
126	    for _, e in ipairs(meta_errs) do errors[#errors + 1] = "meta.json: " .. e end
127	
128	    -- drivers
129	    local drivers = load_drivers(map_dir)
130	
131	    -- boxes
132	    local boxes = {}
133	    local box_files = list_box_files(map_dir .. "/boxes")
134	    for _, path in ipairs(box_files) do
135	        local box, berr = read_json_file(path)
136	        if not box then
137	            errors[#errors + 1] = berr
138	        else
139	            local box_errs = schema.validate_box(box)
140	            if #box_errs > 0 then
141	                for _, e in ipairs(box_errs) do
142	                    errors[#errors + 1] = path .. ": " .. e
143	                end
144	            else
145	                boxes[box.id] = box
146	            end
147	        end
148	    end
149	
150	    if #errors > 0 then return nil, errors end
151	
152	    local graph = {
153	        meta    = meta,
154	        boxes   = boxes,
155	        entry   = meta.entry_box_id,
156	        drivers = drivers,
157	        map_dir = map_dir,
158	    }
159	
160	    return graph, {}
161	end

175	
176	    for id, box in pairs(boxes) do
177	        -- connection integrity: reciprocal check (outgoing connections only)

179	            if c.from_box ~= id then goto next_conn end
180	
181	            local target = boxes[c.to_box]
182	            if target == nil then

186	            end
187	
188	            local found = false
189	            for _, tc in ipairs(target.connections or {}) do
190	                if tc.from_box    == c.from_box   and
191	                   tc.from_output == c.from_output and
192	                   tc.to_box      == c.to_box      and
193	                   tc.to_input    == c.to_input    then
194	                    found = true
195	                    break
196	                end
197	            end
198	            if not found then
199	                errors[#errors + 1] = "box '" .. id .. "': connection " ..
200	                    c.from_box .. "." .. (c.from_output or c.from_port) ..

1	// Box rendering, layout, and hit testing.
2	// Each box knows its world-space position; the camera transform is applied by the canvas.
3	
4	const Boxes = (() => {
5	  const BOX_W         = 180;
6	  const BOX_HEADER    = 36;
7	  const PORT_ROW_H    = 22;
8	  const PORT_R        = 6;   // port dot radius in world units
9	  const MIN_BOX_H     = 80;
10	
11	  const KIND_COLOR = { call: '#4a9eff', branch: '#ff8c42', data: '#4caf7d' };
12	
13	  // boxes: { [id]: box_data }
14	  let boxes = {};
15	
16	  // {{{ box_height
17	  function box_height(box) {
18	    const n = Math.max(
19	      (box.inputs || []).length,
20	      (box.kind === 'branch' ? (box.ports || []).length : (box.outputs || []).length)
21	    );
22	    return Math.max(MIN_BOX_H, BOX_HEADER + n * PORT_ROW_H + 8);
23	  }
24	  // }}}
25	
26	  // {{{ port_positions
27	  // Returns world-space {x,y} for each port dot on a box.
28	  function port_positions(box) {
29	    const bx = box.ui?.x ?? 0;
30	    const by = box.ui?.y ?? 0;
31	    const h  = box_height(box);
32	    const inputs  = box.inputs  || [];
33	    const outputs = box.kind === 'branch' ? (box.ports || []) : (box.outputs || []);
34	
35	    const in_pts  = inputs.map((p, i) => ({
36	      name: p.name, side: 'input',
37	      x: bx,
38	      y: by + BOX_HEADER + PORT_ROW_H * i + PORT_ROW_H / 2,
39	    }));
40	    const out_pts = outputs.map((p, i) => ({
41	      name: p.name, side: 'output',
42	      x: bx + BOX_W,
43	      y: by + BOX_HEADER + PORT_ROW_H * i + PORT_ROW_H / 2,
44	    }));
45	    return { inputs: in_pts, outputs: out_pts };
46	  }
47	  // }}}
48	
49	  // {{{ draw_box
50	  function draw_box(ctx, box, selected) {
51	    const x = box.ui?.x ?? 0;
52	    const y = box.ui?.y ?? 0;
53	    const w = BOX_W;
54	    const h = box_height(box);
55	    const kind  = box.kind || 'call';
56	    const color = KIND_COLOR[kind] || '#888';
57	
58	    // shadow when selected
59	    if (selected) {
60	      ctx.shadowColor = color;
61	      ctx.shadowBlur  = 12;
62	    }
63	
64	    // body
65	    ctx.fillStyle   = '#1e2130';
66	    ctx.strokeStyle = selected ? color : '#3a3f55';
67	    ctx.lineWidth   = selected ? 2 : 1;
68	    ctx.beginPath();
69	    ctx.roundRect(x, y, w, h, 6);
70	    ctx.fill();
71	    ctx.stroke();
72	
73	    ctx.shadowBlur = 0;
74	
75	    // header bar
76	    ctx.fillStyle = color + '33';
77	    ctx.beginPath();
78	    ctx.roundRect(x, y, w, BOX_HEADER, [6, 6, 0, 0]);
79	    ctx.fill();
80	
81	    // label
82	    ctx.fillStyle  = '#e8eaf6';
83	    ctx.font       = 'bold 12px monospace';
84	    ctx.textAlign  = 'left';
85	    ctx.textBaseline = 'middle';
86	    ctx.fillText(box.label || box.id, x + 10, y + BOX_HEADER / 2);
87	
88	    // kind badge (top right)
89	    ctx.fillStyle  = color;
90	    ctx.font       = '9px monospace';
91	    ctx.textAlign  = 'right';
92	    ctx.fillText(kind.toUpperCase(), x + w - 8, y + BOX_HEADER / 2);
93	
94	    // port dots and labels
95	    const { inputs: in_pts, outputs: out_pts } = port_positions(box);
96	
97	    ctx.font      = '10px monospace';
98	    ctx.fillStyle = '#9ea3c0';
99	
100	    in_pts.forEach((p, i) => {
101	      ctx.beginPath();
102	      ctx.arc(p.x, p.y, PORT_R, 0, Math.PI * 2);
103	      ctx.fillStyle   = '#2d3250';
104	      ctx.strokeStyle = '#6c72a0';
105	      ctx.lineWidth   = 1.5;
106	      ctx.fill();
107	      ctx.stroke();
108	
109	      ctx.fillStyle  = '#9ea3c0';
110	      ctx.textAlign  = 'left';
111	      ctx.textBaseline = 'middle';
112	      ctx.fillText(p.name, p.x + PORT_R + 4, p.y);
113	    });
114	
115	    // branch ports use palette colors
116	    const port_palette = ['#ff8c42','#4a9eff','#4caf7d','#e91e63','#9c27b0','#00bcd4'];
117	    out_pts.forEach((p, i) => {
118	      const dot_color = (box.kind === 'branch') ? port_palette[i % port_palette.length] : color;
119	      ctx.beginPath();
120	      ctx.arc(p.x, p.y, PORT_R, 0, Math.PI * 2);
121	      ctx.fillStyle   = dot_color + '44';
122	      ctx.strokeStyle = dot_color;
123	      ctx.lineWidth   = 1.5;
124	      ctx.fill();
125	      ctx.stroke();
126	
127	      ctx.fillStyle  = '#9ea3c0';
128	      ctx.textAlign  = 'right';
129	      ctx.textBaseline = 'middle';
130	      ctx.fillText(p.name, p.x - PORT_R - 4, p.y);
131	    });
132	  }
133	  // }}}
134	
135	  // {{{ draw_all
136	  function draw_all(ctx, selected_id) {
137	    for (const id in boxes) {
138	      draw_box(ctx, boxes[id], id === selected_id);
139	    }
140	  }
141	  // }}}
142	
143	  // {{{ hit_test_port
144	  // Returns {box_id, port_name, side, x, y} for the port under world point (wx,wy), or null.
145	  function hit_test_port(wx, wy) {
146	    for (const id in boxes) {
147	      const { inputs, outputs } = port_positions(boxes[id]);
148	      for (const p of [...inputs, ...outputs]) {
149	        const dx = wx - p.x, dy = wy - p.y;
150	        if (dx*dx + dy*dy <= (PORT_R + 4) * (PORT_R + 4)) {
151	          return { box_id: id, port_name: p.name, side: p.side, x: p.x, y: p.y };
152	        }
153	      }
154	    }
155	    return null;
156	  }
157	  // }}}
158	
159	  // {{{ hit_test_box
160	  // Returns box id if (wx,wy) is inside a box body, or null.
161	  function hit_test_box(wx, wy) {
162	    for (const id in boxes) {
163	      const b = boxes[id];
164	      const x = b.ui?.x ?? 0, y = b.ui?.y ?? 0;
165	      if (wx >= x && wx <= x + BOX_W && wy >= y && wy <= y + box_height(b)) return id;
166	    }
167	    return null;
168	  }
169	  // }}}
170	
171	  // {{{ get_port_world_pos
172	  function get_port_world_pos(box_id, port_name, side) {
173	    const box = boxes[box_id];
174	    if (!box) return null;
175	    const { inputs, outputs } = port_positions(box);
176	    const list = side === 'input' ? inputs : outputs;
177	    const p = list.find(p => p.name === port_name);
178	    return p ? { x: p.x, y: p.y } : null;
179	  }
180	  // }}}
181	
182	  return {
183	    boxes, BOX_W, BOX_HEADER, PORT_R,
184	    box_height, port_positions,
185	    draw_all, draw_box, hit_test_port, hit_test_box, get_port_world_pos,
186	  };
187	})();
188

1	// Wire rendering, hit testing, and connection management.
2	// Wires are derived from box connection data — no separate wire store.
3	
4	const Wires = (() => {
5	  const BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET = 80;
6	  const HIT_SAMPLES        = 20;
7	  const HIT_THRESHOLD_PX   = 8;
8	  const PORT_PALETTE = ['#ff8c42','#4a9eff','#4caf7d','#e91e63','#9c27b0','#00bcd4'];
9	
10	  let _selected_wire = null;  // { from_box, from_port, to_box, to_input }
11	
12	  // {{{ selected_wire
13	  function selected_wire() { return _selected_wire; }
14	  // }}}
15	
16	  // {{{ bezier_point
17	  function bezier_point(t, x0, y0, cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1, x1, y1) {
18	    const mt = 1 - t;
19	    return {
20	      x: mt*mt*mt*x0 + 3*mt*mt*t*cx0 + 3*mt*t*t*cx1 + t*t*t*x1,
21	      y: mt*mt*mt*y0 + 3*mt*mt*t*cy0 + 3*mt*t*t*cy1 + t*t*t*y1,
22	    };
23	  }
24	  // }}}
25	
26	  // {{{ draw_bezier
27	  function draw_bezier(ctx, x0, y0, x1, y1, color, selected) {
28	    const cx0 = x0 + BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET;
29	    const cy0 = y0;
30	    const cx1 = x1 - BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET;
31	    const cy1 = y1;
32	
33	    ctx.beginPath();
34	    ctx.moveTo(x0, y0);
35	    ctx.bezierCurveTo(cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1, x1, y1);
36	    ctx.strokeStyle = selected ? '#ffffff' : color;
37	    ctx.lineWidth   = selected ? 2.5 : 1.5;
38	    ctx.setLineDash(selected ? [] : []);
39	    ctx.stroke();
40	  }
41	  // }}}
42	
43	  // {{{ hit_test_wire
44	  // Returns the connection record if cursor (wx,wy) is within HIT_THRESHOLD pixels
45	  // of any wire, or null. Operates in world space; threshold is adjusted for zoom.
46	  function hit_test_wire(wx, wy, zoom) {
47	    const threshold = HIT_THRESHOLD_PX / zoom;
48	    for (const id in Boxes.boxes) {
49	      const box = Boxes.boxes[id];
50	      for (const c of box.connections || []) {
51	        if (c.from_box !== id) continue;
52	
53	        const src  = Boxes.get_port_world_pos(c.from_box, c.from_output || c.from_port, 'output');
54	        const dst  = Boxes.get_port_world_pos(c.to_box,   c.to_input,                   'input');
55	        if (!src || !dst) continue;
56	
57	        const cx0 = src.x + BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET, cy0 = src.y;
58	        const cx1 = dst.x - BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET, cy1 = dst.y;
59	
60	        for (let i = 0; i <= HIT_SAMPLES; i++) {
61	          const t = i / HIT_SAMPLES;
62	          const p = bezier_point(t, src.x, src.y, cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1, dst.x, dst.y);
63	          const dx = wx - p.x, dy = wy - p.y;
64	          if (dx*dx + dy*dy <= threshold*threshold) return c;
65	        }
66	      }
67	    }
68	    return null;
69	  }
70	  // }}}
71	
72	  // {{{ draw_all
73	  function draw_all(ctx, selected_box_id) {
74	    let branch_port_index = {};
75	
76	    for (const id in Boxes.boxes) {
77	      const box = Boxes.boxes[id];
78	      for (const c of box.connections || []) {
79	        if (c.from_box !== id) continue;
80	
81	        const src = Boxes.get_port_world_pos(c.from_box, c.from_output || c.from_port, 'output');
82	        const dst = Boxes.get_port_world_pos(c.to_box,   c.to_input,                   'input');
83	        if (!src || !dst) continue;
84	
85	        // branch and llm-route boxes get per-port palette colors
86	        let color = '#4a6080';
87	        if (box.kind === 'branch' || box.kind === 'llm-route') {
88	          const port_name = c.from_port;
89	          if (!branch_port_index[id]) branch_port_index[id] = {};
90	          if (branch_port_index[id][port_name] === undefined) {
91	            branch_port_index[id][port_name] = Object.keys(branch_port_index[id]).length;
92	          }
93	          color = PORT_PALETTE[branch_port_index[id][port_name] % PORT_PALETTE.length];
94	        }
95	
96	        const is_selected = _selected_wire &&
97	          _selected_wire.from_box    === c.from_box &&
98	          _selected_wire.from_output === c.from_output &&
99	          _selected_wire.to_box      === c.to_box &&
100	          _selected_wire.to_input    === c.to_input;
101	
102	        draw_bezier(ctx, src.x, src.y, dst.x, dst.y, color, is_selected);
103	      }
104	    }

33	    local tmp = path .. ".tmp"
34	    local f, err = io.open(tmp, "w")
35	    if not f then return false, err end
36	    f:write(data)
37	    f:close()
38	    local ok, rerr = os.rename(tmp, path)
39	    if not ok then return false, rerr end

57	    if not handle then return {} end

59	    handle:close()

71	    if not handle then return {} end
72	    local listing = handle:read("*a")
73	    handle:close()
74	    local names = {}

76	        local name = path:match("/([^/]+)/$")
77	        if name then names[#names + 1] = name end

## Assistant

The server calls `schema.validate_box` which now uses the new `from_branch` schema — that's consistent.

Everything is wired up. Here's a summary of what changed:

- **Schema** (`001-schema.lua`): removed `branch` kind, `valid_ops`, all branch/predicate validators. Connections now use `from_branch` (nil or "lt"/"eq"/"gt"). Call boxes have no `outputs`, optional `comparand` string.

- **Loader** (`003-loader.lua`): removed branch-box cycle exemption (all cycles are errors now). Reciprocal check uses `from_branch`. Removed branch-else validation.

- **Executor** (`004-executor.lua`): removed `eval_predicate`, `llm_route_call`, retry machinery. `run_task` returns a single value (first array element). `fire_connections` routes that value either through a comparator (number check → lt/eq/gt) or straight through to all nil-branched connections.

- **Boxes** (`002-boxes.js`): one output dot centered vertically; if `box.comparand` set, three dots (lt/eq/gt) with distinct colors and labels. Removed branch kind color.

- **Inspector** (`004-inspector.js`): removed `branch`/`llm-route` kinds and their entire UI sections. Input port names are read-only labels. Output section shows a "compare" toggle; when active, shows a number input for the comparand.

- **Wires** (`006-wires.js`): all connection records use `from_branch`. Wire color comes from branch name. Dup check and deletion match on `from_branch`.

- **App** (`005-app.js`): new boxes no longer get `outputs: []`.

## User

Can you tell me what happens when multiple output wires are sent to the same input wire on a separate box? Also, we need a way to delete wires.

## Assistant

**Multiple wires to the same input:** the store is a flat key-value table keyed by `"box_id.port_name"`. If two upstream boxes both fire connections to the same input (e.g., both write to `C.x`), it's last-write-wins — whichever upstream box runs last in the queue overwrites the entry. The target box only executes once (the `visited` guard), so it receives whichever value happened to arrive last. There's no merging or error — it silently drops one. Wiring two outputs to the same input is probably a mistake and ideally would be caught at load time, but currently isn't.

**Wire deletion** is already implemented — click a wire to select it (it turns white), then press Delete or Backspace. The mechanism works but it's invisible. Let me make it obvious by showing a status hint when a wire is selected:

## Assistant

**On the LLM decoupling question:** it's essentially complete. The executor has zero LLM-specific code — `llm_route_call` and `eval_predicate` are gone. The inspector has no LLM box type. Wires have no LLM awareness. The only remaining artifact is `libs/ollama.lua`, which is just a library — it's no different from any other helper a Lua function might `require`. A box that needs an LLM call writes a Lua function that calls `require("ollama")`, selects it via the file browser, and the executor calls it identically to any other box. The coupling is fully severed at the architecture level.

**Summary of what just changed:**
- **Delete box**: now strips all incoming connection records from other boxes before calling DELETE — no more 409, no confirmation dialog
- **+ box tool**: click button → cursor becomes `copy` → next canvas click places a box → back to select mode; double-click removed
- **✕ wire tool**: click button → cursor becomes `cell` → drag across wires to erase them → mouse up returns to select mode
- **Issues 212 and 213** created

83	-- {{{ safe_path
84	-- Returns the resolved path if it is within root, nil otherwise.
85	-- Prevents path traversal outside the maps root.
86	local function safe_path(root, ...)
87	    local parts = { root }
88	    for _, p in ipairs({...}) do
89	        -- reject any component containing ".."
90	        if tostring(p):find("%.%.", 1, true) then return nil end
91	        parts[#parts + 1] = p
92	    end
93	    return table.concat(parts, "/")
94	end

## Assistant

All clean. `req.path` is now the clean path without query string, which is exactly what the static file serving and the logging need. `req.query` is only used by the new `handle_list_dirs` handler.

Here's what changed:

- **Server**: `parse_request` now splits query strings from the path before building `parts` — previous handlers are unaffected since they only use `parts`. New `GET /fs/dirs?path=...` route lists immediate subdirectories of any absolute path on the server filesystem.

- **API**: `list_dirs(path)` added.

- **File browser**: Clicking "+ library dir" now opens an in-panel directory navigator instead of a `prompt()`. It starts at `/home`, shows subdirectories as clickable rows, has a `..` row for going up, and a "✓ use this directory" button that saves the selection. The back button cancels and returns to the file list.

Done. Here's what changed:

**Editable box name:**
- The inspector title (`#inspector-title`) is now an editable input showing `box.label` — changing it updates the label and autosaves, which triggers canvas redraw via the `on_change_cb`
- The box ID appears as a small dim subtitle underneath
- The separate "label" field in the inspector body is removed (it was redundant)
- Canvas already displayed `box.label`, so that works automatically once the label is changed from "New Box"

**Map picker:**
- Clicking the map name ("hello") in the sidebar header opens the inspector panel with the map picker
- Shows two collapsible groups: "recent" (from localStorage history, up to 20 entries) and "on this server" (from `API.list_maps()`)
- Recent entries from different servers show the server URL as a dim subtitle
- "Open map by name" section at the bottom has a text input + button for switching to any map by name
- Switching saves to history and reloads

**Right-click + export:**
- `contextmenu` event on the canvas calls `e.preventDefault()` — browser context menu is suppressed

```
-- Ollama API client — shared library and ready-to-use box source.
-- require("ollama") from any Lua function, or copy to a map's src/ directory
-- to use it directly as a call box via the file browser.
-- Uses curl; no extra Lua packages needed beyond dkjson.

local json = require("dkjson")
local M    = {}

local DEFAULT_HOST  = "http://localhost:11434"
local DEFAULT_MODEL = "llama3.2"

-- {{{ shell_quote
local function shell_quote(s)
    return "'" .. tostring(s):gsub("'", "'\\''") .. "'"
end
-- }}}

-- {{{ curl_post
local function curl_post(url, payload)
    local cmd = "curl -s --max-time 120 -X POST " .. shell_quote(url) ..
                " -H " .. shell_quote("Content-Type: application/json") ..
                " -d " .. shell_quote(payload)
    local ph  = io.popen(cmd)
    local out = ph:read("*a")
    ph:close()
    return out
end
-- }}}

-- {{{ M.query
-- Plain text generation. model and host are optional; nil or empty uses defaults.
function M.query(prompt, model, host)
    model = (model ~= nil and model ~= "") and model or DEFAULT_MODEL
    host  = (host  ~= nil and host  ~= "") and host  or DEFAULT_HOST
    local out  = curl_post(host .. "/api/generate", json.encode({
        model  = model,
        prompt = tostring(prompt),
        stream = false,
    }))
    local resp, _, err = json.decode(out)
    if not resp then
        io.stderr:write("ollama.query: bad response: " .. tostring(err) .. "\n")
        return nil
    end
    if resp.error then
        io.stderr:write("ollama.query: " .. tostring(resp.error) .. "\n")
        return nil
    end
    return resp.response
end
-- }}}

-- {{{ M.chat
-- Chat-style query. system prompt is optional; nil or empty omits it.
-- model and host default when nil or empty.
function M.chat(prompt, system, model, host)
    model = (model ~= nil and model ~= "") and model or DEFAULT_MODEL
    host  = (host  ~= nil and host  ~= "") and host  or DEFAULT_HOST
    local messages = {}
    if system ~= nil and system ~= "" then
        messages[#messages + 1] = { role = "system", content = tostring(system) }
    end
    messages[#messages + 1] = { role = "user", content = tostring(prompt) }
    local out  = curl_post(host .. "/api/chat", json.encode({
        model    = model,
        messages = messages,
        stream   = false,
    }))
    local resp, _, err = json.decode(out)
    if not resp then
        io.stderr:write("ollama.chat: bad response: " .. tostring(err) .. "\n")
        return nil
    end
    if resp.error then
        io.stderr:write("ollama.chat: " .. tostring(resp.error) .. "\n")
        return nil
    end
    return resp.message and resp.message.content or nil
end
-- }}}

return M
```

**LLM libraries:** `libs/ollama.lua` is at `/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/libs/`. It already has public functions (`M.query`, `M.chat`) in the `function M.name()` format. **Call vs Data:** Currently they're functionally identical — both go through the same executor path (`run_task`). `data` was originally intended as a semantic label for "this box provides constant input" vs "this box computes something," but literal port values on any input port now covers that use case. The distinction is just documentary at this point. You can safely use `call` for everything.

1. Primary Request and Intent:

The session continued development of SoraMech — a visual FSM/dataflow editor. The primary requests in chronological order were:

1. **Update issue files** to reflect the new single-wire-output + comparator architecture (replacing branch/llm-route box kinds)
2. **Implement the new architecture**: one output wire per box, optional comparator (lt/eq/gt split), `from_branch` connection field, input names read-only from parsing, error on non-number comparison

2. Key Technical Concepts:
- SoraMech: visual dataflow editor — boxes, wires, maps (directories), server (Lua HTTP), runner/executor (Lua FSM), browser editor (vanilla JS canvas)
- **Single-wire output model**: every box has exactly one output wire carrying the raw first return value; no named output ports
- **Comparator**: optional `box.comparand` string; if set, output wire splits into lt/eq/gt; non-number output is a hard error
- **Connection schema**: `{from_box, from_branch (null|"lt"|"eq"|"gt"), to_box, to_input}` — replaces old `from_output`/`from_port` dual-field system
- **Store model**: flat `"box_id.port_name" → value` table pre-seeded with literal port values; wires overwrite
- **File browser**: parses `function M.name(args)` Lua pattern; `lua_fn_end()` nesting tracker; bash `name() {}` pattern
- **Tool modes**: `select` | `erase-wire` (was also `add-box`, now removed in favor of right-click)
- **Context menu**: dynamically created floating div, positioned at cursor, context-sensitive items
- **Server query string parsing**: `parse_request` now splits `?` from path before building parts; `parse_query` decodes URL-encoded params; `req.query` is a key→value table
- **Map history**: localStorage `soramech_maps_history` array of `{server_url, map_name}`, deduplicated, max 20 entries
- LuaJIT-compatible syntax throughout; dkjson for JSON; luasocket for HTTP server

4. Errors and fixes:
- **Server 409 on box delete** ("box 'X' is referenced by: greet, write-result"): The server's `find_references` function scans all boxes for connections referencing the target box and returns 409 if any found. Fixed client-side: `delete_box` now fetches every other box, strips connections referencing the deleted box, PUTs them back, then calls DELETE.
- **`from_output`/`from_port` dual-field system**: Old connection schema used `from_output` for call boxes and `from_port` for branch/llm-route. Replaced with unified `from_branch` (null or lt/eq/gt) across all files.
- **Server query string parsing**: `parse_request` previously included `?query=string` in the path segments. Fixed by splitting at `?` before building parts array.
- **Duplicate `#map-label` CSS rule**: Added cursor/hover styling in a new block, then found the original block at line 55. Merged into original rule and removed duplicate.

5. Problem Solving:
- **Single-wire output**: Clean design — one output value flows through, comparator optionally branches. `port_positions` returns null-named single dot or three named branch dots. `get_port_world_pos(box_id, null, 'output')` works because `find(p => p.name === null)` matches.
- **Erase-wire during drag**: `erase_seen` Set of `wire_sig(c)` strings prevents double-deletion during a single drag. Local cache updated immediately for visual feedback; async DELETE called in background.
- **Map picker history**: Deduplicated array in localStorage, prepends most recent, capped at 20. `switch_to_map` handles both same-server and cross-server switching.
- **Context menu off-screen**: After `show`, reads `getBoundingClientRect()` and adjusts left/top if menu would overflow viewport.
- **LLM decoupling**: Fully complete — executor, inspector, and wires have zero LLM-specific code. `libs/ollama.lua` is just a regular Lua library with `M.query`/`M.chat` public functions; add `libs/` as a library directory in the file browser to use them.

1	// Wire rendering, hit testing, and connection management.
2	// Wires are derived from box connection data — no separate wire store.
3	
4	const Wires = (() => {
5	  const BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET = 80;
6	  const HIT_SAMPLES        = 20;
7	  const HIT_THRESHOLD_PX   = 8;
8	
9	  // Colors for comparator branch wires; plain wire uses default
10	  const BRANCH_COLOR = { lt: '#ff8c42', eq: '#4a9eff', gt: '#4caf7d' };
11	
12	  let _selected_wire = null;  // { from_box, from_branch, to_box, to_input }
13	
14	  // {{{ selected_wire
15	  function selected_wire() { return _selected_wire; }
16	  // }}}
17	
18	  // {{{ bezier_point
19	  function bezier_point(t, x0, y0, cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1, x1, y1) {
20	    const mt = 1 - t;
21	    return {
22	      x: mt*mt*mt*x0 + 3*mt*mt*t*cx0 + 3*mt*t*t*cx1 + t*t*t*x1,
23	      y: mt*mt*mt*y0 + 3*mt*mt*t*cy0 + 3*mt*t*t*cy1 + t*t*t*y1,
24	    };
25	  }
26	  // }}}
27	
28	  // {{{ draw_bezier
29	  function draw_bezier(ctx, x0, y0, x1, y1, color, selected) {
30	    const cx0 = x0 + BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET;
31	    const cy0 = y0;
32	    const cx1 = x1 - BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET;
33	    const cy1 = y1;
34	
35	    ctx.beginPath();
36	    ctx.moveTo(x0, y0);
37	    ctx.bezierCurveTo(cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1, x1, y1);
38	    ctx.strokeStyle = selected ? '#ffffff' : color;
39	    ctx.lineWidth   = selected ? 2.5 : 1.5;
40	    ctx.setLineDash([]);
41	    ctx.stroke();
42	  }
43	  // }}}
44	
45	  // {{{ hit_test_wire
46	  // Returns the connection record if cursor (wx,wy) is within HIT_THRESHOLD pixels
47	  // of any wire, or null. Operates in world space; threshold is adjusted for zoom.
48	  function hit_test_wire(wx, wy, zoom) {
49	    const threshold = HIT_THRESHOLD_PX / zoom;
50	    for (const id in Boxes.boxes) {
51	      const box = Boxes.boxes[id];
52	      for (const c of box.connections || []) {
53	        if (c.from_box !== id) continue;
54	
55	        const src  = Boxes.get_port_world_pos(c.from_box, c.from_branch ?? null, 'output');
56	        const dst  = Boxes.get_port_world_pos(c.to_box,   c.to_input,            'input');
57	        if (!src || !dst) continue;
58	
59	        const cx0 = src.x + BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET, cy0 = src.y;
60	        const cx1 = dst.x - BEZIER_CTRL_OFFSET, cy1 = dst.y;
61	
62	        for (let i = 0; i <= HIT_SAMPLES; i++) {
63	          const t = i / HIT_SAMPLES;
64	          const p = bezier_point(t, src.x, src.y, cx0, cy0, cx1, cy1, dst.x, dst.y);
65	          const dx = wx - p.x, dy = wy - p.y;
66	          if (dx*dx + dy*dy <= threshold*threshold) return c;
67	        }
68	      }
69	    }
70	    return null;
71	  }
72	  // }}}
73	
74	  // {{{ draw_all
75	  function draw_all(ctx, selected_box_id) {
76	    for (const id in Boxes.boxes) {
77	      const box = Boxes.boxes[id];

79	        if (c.from_box !== id) continue;

81	        const src = Boxes.get_port_world_pos(c.from_box, c.from_branch ?? null, 'output');
82	        const dst = Boxes.get_port_world_pos(c.to_box,   c.to_input,            'input');
83	        if (!src || !dst) continue;
84	
85	        // comparator branches get distinct colors; plain wire uses dim default
86	        const color = c.from_branch ? (BRANCH_COLOR[c.from_branch] || '#4a6080') : '#4a6080';
87	
88	        const is_selected = _selected_wire &&
89	          _selected_wire.from_box    === c.from_box &&
90	          _selected_wire.from_branch === c.from_branch &&
91	          _selected_wire.to_box      === c.to_box &&
92	          _selected_wire.to_input    === c.to_input;
93	
94	        draw_bezier(ctx, src.x, src.y, dst.x, dst.y, color, is_selected);
95	      }
96	    }
97	  }
98	  // }}}

1	// Box rendering, layout, and hit testing.
2	// Each box knows its world-space position; the camera transform is applied by the canvas.
3	
4	const Boxes = (() => {
5	  const BOX_W         = 180;
6	  const BOX_HEADER    = 36;
7	  const PORT_ROW_H    = 22;
8	  const PORT_R        = 6;   // port dot radius in world units
9	  const MIN_BOX_H     = 80;
10	
11	  const KIND_COLOR = { call: '#4a9eff', data: '#4caf7d' };
12	
13	  // Comparator branch colors: lt=orange, eq=blue, gt=green
14	  const BRANCH_COLOR = { lt: '#ff8c42', eq: '#4a9eff', gt: '#4caf7d' };
15	
16	  // boxes: { [id]: box_data }
17	  let boxes = {};
18	
19	  // {{{ box_height
20	  function box_height(box) {
21	    const n_in  = (box.inputs || []).length;
22	    // comparator active: 3 output dots (lt/eq/gt); otherwise: 1 centered dot
23	    const n_out = (box.comparand && box.comparand !== '') ? 3 : 1;
24	    return Math.max(MIN_BOX_H, BOX_HEADER + Math.max(n_in, n_out) * PORT_ROW_H + 8);
25	  }
26	  // }}}
27	
28	  // {{{ port_positions
29	  // Returns world-space {x,y} for each port dot on a box.
30	  // Output side: null-named single dot when no comparand; 'lt'/'eq'/'gt' dots when comparand set.
31	  function port_positions(box) {
32	    const bx = box.ui?.x ?? 0;
33	    const by = box.ui?.y ?? 0;
34	    const h  = box_height(box);
35	    const inputs = box.inputs || [];
36	
37	    const in_pts = inputs.map((p, i) => ({
38	      name: p.name, side: 'input',
39	      x: bx,
40	      y: by + BOX_HEADER + PORT_ROW_H * i + PORT_ROW_H / 2,
41	    }));
42	
43	    let out_pts;
44	    if (box.comparand && box.comparand !== '') {
45	      // three comparator dots
46	      out_pts = ['lt', 'eq', 'gt'].map((branch, i) => ({
47	        name: branch, side: 'output',
48	        x: bx + BOX_W,
49	        y: by + BOX_HEADER + PORT_ROW_H * i + PORT_ROW_H / 2,
50	      }));
51	    } else {
52	      // single output dot, vertically centered in the box
53	      out_pts = [{
54	        name: null, side: 'output',
55	        x: bx + BOX_W,
56	        y: by + h / 2,
57	      }];
58	    }
59	
60	    return { inputs: in_pts, outputs: out_pts };
61	  }
62	  // }}}
63	
64	  // {{{ draw_box
65	  function draw_box(ctx, box, selected) {
66	    const x = box.ui?.x ?? 0;
67	    const y = box.ui?.y ?? 0;
68	    const w = BOX_W;
69	    const h = box_height(box);
70	    const kind  = box.kind || 'call';
71	    const color = KIND_COLOR[kind] || '#888';
72	
73	    // shadow when selected
74	    if (selected) {
75	      ctx.shadowColor = color;
76	      ctx.shadowBlur  = 12;
77	    }
78	
79	    // body
80	    ctx.fillStyle   = '#1e2130';
81	    ctx.strokeStyle = selected ? color : '#3a3f55';
82	    ctx.lineWidth   = selected ? 2 : 1;
83	    ctx.beginPath();
84	    ctx.roundRect(x, y, w, h, 6);
85	    ctx.fill();
86	    ctx.stroke();
87	
88	    ctx.shadowBlur = 0;
89	
90	    // header bar
91	    ctx.fillStyle = color + '33';
92	    ctx.beginPath();
93	    ctx.roundRect(x, y, w, BOX_HEADER, [6, 6, 0, 0]);
94	    ctx.fill();
95	
96	    // label
97	    ctx.fillStyle  = '#e8eaf6';
98	    ctx.font       = 'bold 12px monospace';
99	    ctx.textAlign  = 'left';
100	    ctx.textBaseline = 'middle';
101	    ctx.fillText(box.label || box.id, x + 10, y + BOX_HEADER / 2);
102	
103	    // kind badge (top right)
104	    ctx.fillStyle  = color;
105	    ctx.font       = '9px monospace';
106	    ctx.textAlign  = 'right';
107	    ctx.fillText(kind.toUpperCase(), x + w - 8, y + BOX_HEADER / 2);
108	
109	    // port dots and labels
110	    const { inputs: in_pts, outputs: out_pts } = port_positions(box);
111	
112	    ctx.font      = '10px monospace';
113	    ctx.fillStyle = '#9ea3c0';
114	
115	    in_pts.forEach(p => {
116	      ctx.beginPath();
117	      ctx.arc(p.x, p.y, PORT_R, 0, Math.PI * 2);
118	      ctx.fillStyle   = '#2d3250';
119	      ctx.strokeStyle = '#6c72a0';
120	      ctx.lineWidth   = 1.5;
121	      ctx.fill();
122	      ctx.stroke();
123	
124	      ctx.fillStyle  = '#9ea3c0';
125	      ctx.textAlign  = 'left';
126	      ctx.textBaseline = 'middle';
127	      ctx.fillText(p.name, p.x + PORT_R + 4, p.y);
128	    });
129	
130	    out_pts.forEach(p => {
131	      // comparator branches use distinct colors; single output uses kind color
132	      const dot_color = p.name ? (BRANCH_COLOR[p.name] || color) : color;
133	      ctx.beginPath();
134	      ctx.arc(p.x, p.y, PORT_R, 0, Math.PI * 2);
135	      ctx.fillStyle   = dot_color + '44';
136	      ctx.strokeStyle = dot_color;
137	      ctx.lineWidth   = 1.5;
138	      ctx.fill();
139	      ctx.stroke();
140	
141	      // label branch name ('lt'/'eq'/'gt') to the left of the dot; nothing for single wire
142	      if (p.name) {
143	        ctx.fillStyle  = dot_color;
144	        ctx.font       = '9px monospace';
145	        ctx.textAlign  = 'right';
146	        ctx.textBaseline = 'middle';
147	        ctx.fillText(p.name, p.x - PORT_R - 4, p.y);
148	      }
149	    });
150	  }
151	  // }}}
152	
153	  // {{{ draw_all
154	  function draw_all(ctx, selected_id) {
155	    for (const id in boxes) {
156	      draw_box(ctx, boxes[id], id === selected_id);
157	    }
158	  }
159	  // }}}
160	
161	  // {{{ hit_test_port
162	  // Returns {box_id, port_name, side, x, y} for the port under world point (wx,wy), or null.
163	  // For output dots: port_name is null (single wire) or 'lt'/'eq'/'gt' (comparator).
164	  function hit_test_port(wx, wy) {
165	    for (const id in boxes) {
166	      const { inputs, outputs } = port_positions(boxes[id]);
167	      for (const p of [...inputs, ...outputs]) {
168	        const dx = wx - p.x, dy = wy - p.y;
169	        if (dx*dx + dy*dy <= (PORT_R + 4) * (PORT_R + 4)) {
170	          return { box_id: id, port_name: p.name, side: p.side, x: p.x, y: p.y };
171	        }
172	      }
173	    }
174	    return null;
175	  }
176	  // }}}
177	
178	  // {{{ hit_test_box
179	  // Returns box id if (wx,wy) is inside a box body, or null.
180	  function hit_test_box(wx, wy) {
181	    for (const id in boxes) {
182	      const b = boxes[id];
183	      const x = b.ui?.x ?? 0, y = b.ui?.y ?? 0;
184	      if (wx >= x && wx <= x + BOX_W && wy >= y && wy <= y + box_height(b)) return id;
185	    }
186	    return null;
187	  }
188	  // }}}
189	
190	  // {{{ get_port_world_pos
191	  function get_port_world_pos(box_id, port_name, side) {

## Assistant

When a literal value is typed into an input port field, the handler now:

1. Finds all connections where `to_box` is the current box and `to_input` matches the port
2. Removes them from `current_box.connections` locally before calling `save()` — so the PUT reflects the clean state
3. Fetches and updates each source box to remove its side of the connection record
4. Redraws the canvas so the severed wires disappear immediately
5. Shows a status message counting how many wires were cut

Clearing the field back to empty restores the port to "wire-fed" mode — the wires won't reappear automatically, but new ones can be drawn.
